# Brown-Driver-Briggs Hebrew Lexicon

## ה (id: BDB02243)

ה, *Hē*, fifth letter; \= numeral 5 in post B. Heb.; no evidence of this usage in OT times.



## הַ (id: BDB02244)

•הַ, הַ, הָ, הֶ (on the use of these different forms, see the Grammars: e.g. Ges§ 35\), **definite article,** the (so Moab. Ph. (Schröd:§ 62–4\), Liḥyan (NW. Arabia) *ha* (DHMEpigr. Denkm. 4\. 13\. 58 ff.); not As. Aram. or Eth.: Arab. أَلْ, of which, before dentals, sibilants, and liquids, the *l* is written but not pronounced, thus أَلشَّمْسُ pron. *’ash\-shamsu* \= Heb. הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ)—in gen. the use of the art. in Heb. is analogous to its use in Greek or German: but naturally there are applications peculiar to Hebrew (comp. with what follows Ges§ 126 Ew§ 277\):—**1\.** joined with *substantives:* **a.** to mark a definite concrete object, as [Gn 1:1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1) *the* heavens and *the* earth, הַמֶּלֶח the king, etc. Never, however (as in Greek e.g. ὁ Πλάτων), before true proper names, though it is used with certain terms, chiefly geographical, of which the orig. appellative sense has not been lost, as הַבַּעַל ‘Baal,’ lit. ‘ the lord,’ in pl. הַבְּעָלִים i.e. the various local Baals, הַשָּטָן *the* Adversary [Jb 1:6](https://ref.ly/Job1:6) ff. (as a pr. n. שָטָן, ‘Satan,’ only [1 Ch 21:1](https://ref.ly/1Chr21:1)); הַלְּבָנוֺן (but not הַחֶרְמוֺן), הַיַּרְדֵּן (but not הָאַרְנֹן), הַכַּרְמֶל, הַשָּׁרוֺן the Sharon, הַשְּׁפֵלָה the (Judaean) lowland, הַכִּכָּר, הַמִּישׁוֺר the (Moabite) table\-land, הָעֲרָבָה, הָעַי ‘ïAi,’ הַגִּבְעָה ‘Gibeah,’ Ju [20:5](https://ref.ly/Judg20:5) ff., הַגָּלִיל ‘Galilee,’ הַגִּלְגָּל, הַגִּלְבֹּעַ, הַבָּשָׁן, הַגִּלְעָד (oft.), הַשִּׁטִּים, הַמִּחְפָּה, הָרָמָה, הַיְשִׁימוֺן, הָעֹפֶל, הַפִּסְגָּה. **b.** with an adjective to denote one who exhibits a quality κατ̓ ἐξοχήν, i.e. to express the compar. or superl. degree: so oft., as [Gn 1:16](https://ref.ly/Gen1:16) המאור הגדל the *greater* light, המאור הקטן the *lesser* light, [27:1](https://ref.ly/Gen27:1) בנו הגדול \= his *elder* son, [48:14](https://ref.ly/Gen48:14) הצעיר the *younger*, [42:13](https://ref.ly/Gen42:13) הַקָּטֹן the little one, i.e. the *youngest*, (of Joseph's brethren), [Lv 21:10](https://ref.ly/Lev21:10) הַגָּדוֺל מֵאֶחָיו the *chiefest* of his brethren, [Nu 35:25](https://ref.ly/Num35:25) \+ הכהן הגדול the *chief* priest, ´הצעיר ב the *least* among Ju [6:15](https://ref.ly/Judg6:15), הצעירה מן the *least* of 1S [9:21](https://ref.ly/1Sam9:21), ´הַיָּפָה ב the *fairest* among Ct [1:8](https://ref.ly/Song1:8), [2 K 10:3](https://ref.ly/2Kgs10:3), [Pr 30:30](https://ref.ly/Prov30:30), cf. [Job 14:15](https://ref.ly/Job14:15). **c.** with nouns which are not definite in themselves, but *acquire their definition from the context, or from the manner in which they are introduced:* thus (α) in the standing phrases הַיּוֺם *to\-day*, [Gn 4:14](https://ref.ly/Gen4:14), [21:26](https://ref.ly/Gen21:26) \+ oft.; הַלַּיְלָה *to\-night*, [Gn 19:5](https://ref.ly/Gen19:5), [30:15](https://ref.ly/Gen30:15) \+, once [1 S 15:16](https://ref.ly/1Sam15:16) *last night;* so הַשָּׁנָה *this* year, [2 K 19:29](https://ref.ly/2Kgs19:29), [Jer 28:16](https://ref.ly/Jer28:16); הַפַּעַם *this* time, [Gn 18:32](https://ref.ly/Gen18:32) \+. (β) הַנָּהָר *the* river (κατ̓ ἐξοχήν), i.e. the Euphrates; [Ex 2:15](https://ref.ly/Exod2:15) *the well*, the well viz. of the district, [Jos 8:11](https://ref.ly/Josh8:11) *the* valley, [1 S 17:3](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:3); [1 S 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Sam19:10) *the* wall, v:[13](https://ref.ly/1Sam19:13) *the* bed, [20:21](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:21) הַנַּעַר *the* lad (whom Jonathan would naturally take with him), v:[34](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:34) *the* table. Hence occas. where a suffix would define the noun more precisely, as הַחֲמוֺר [2 S 19:27](https://ref.ly/2Sam19:27) \+ \= *my* ass, Ju [3:20](https://ref.ly/Judg3:20), 1 S [1:9](https://ref.ly/2Sam1:9) הַכִּסֵּא \= *his* seat, Ju [4:15](https://ref.ly/Judg4:15), [1 K 22:35](https://ref.ly/1Kgs22:35), [2 K 10:15](https://ref.ly/2Kgs10:15) \+ הַמֶּרְכָּבָה, [1 S 18:10](https://ref.ly/1Sam18:10), [20:33](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:33) הַחֲנִית. **d.** it is a peculiarity of Hebrew thought to conceive an object as defined by its being *taken for a particular purpose*, and thus by a kind of prolepsis to prefix the art. to the noun denoting it: [1 S 10:1](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:1) and Samuel took אֶת־פַּח הַשֶּׁמֶן lit. *the* cruse of oil, not, however, a cruse which had been defined previously, but one rendered definite by *being now taken;* in English idiom ‘ *a* cruse of oil,’ v:[25](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:25) בַּסֵּפֶר lit. in *the* scroll or book, the one, viz. taken for the purpose, i.e. in *a* scroll (so [Ex 17:14](https://ref.ly/Exod17:14), [Nu 5:23](https://ref.ly/Num5:23), [Jb 19:23](https://ref.ly/Job19:23)), [21:10](https://ref.ly/Job21:10) בַּשִּמְלָה, Ju [4:18](https://ref.ly/Judg4:18) וַתְּכַסֵּהוּ בַּשְּמִיכָה, v:[19](https://ref.ly/Judg4:19), [7:13](https://ref.ly/Judg7:13) הָאֹהֶל *a* tent, [8:25](https://ref.ly/Judg8:25), [9:48](https://ref.ly/Judg9:48) אֶת־הַקַּרְדֻּמֹּת *hatchets*, [20:16](https://ref.ly/Judg20:16) every one able to sling בָּאֶבֶן אֶל־הַשַּעֲרָה with *a* stone at *a* hair, [1 S 6:8](https://ref.ly/1Sam6:8) בָּאַרְגָּז (unless indeed the אַרְגָּז was an understood appendage in every cart), [Nu 11:27](https://ref.ly/Num11:27) הַנַּעַר, *a* young man, [13:23](https://ref.ly/Num13:23) בַּמּוֺט on *a* pole, [Jos 2:15](https://ref.ly/Josh2:15) בַּחֶבֶל with *a* cord, [2 S 17:17](https://ref.ly/2Sam17:17) השׁפחה *a* girl (cf. Dr:[1 S 1:4](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:4), [19:13](https://ref.ly/1Sam19:13)). Sts. it is uncertain whether an art. is to be referred to **c** or **d**: e.g. [1 S 2:13](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:13) *his* prong or *a* prong, [2 S 18:9](https://ref.ly/2Sam18:9) *his* mule or *a* mule, etc. **e.** with nouns that denote *objects or classes of objects that are known to all*, as הַצּאֹן, הַזָּהָב, הַמַּיִם; [Gn 13:2](https://ref.ly/Gen13:2) Abram was very rich בַּמִּקְנֶה בַּכֶּסֶף וּבַזָּהָב, [Ex 31:4](https://ref.ly/Exod31:4) לַעֲשוֺת בַּזָּהָב וּבַכֶּסֶף, [Dt 14:26](https://ref.ly/Deut14:26) and thou shalt lay out the money בַּבָּקָר וּבַצּאֹן וּבַיַּיִן וּבַשֵּׁכָר, [2 K 9:30](https://ref.ly/2Kgs9:30) וַתָּשֶם בַּפּוּח עֵינֶיהָ, in French ‘elle mit *du* fard à ses yeux.’ It is, however, remarkable that this usage depends mostly on the punctuation, הַכֶּסֶף, הַיַּיִן, הַלֶּחֶם etc. (except as applied to denote *definite* quantities of gold, wine, etc., as [Jos 6:24](https://ref.ly/Josh6:24)) being far less common than כֶּסֶף, יַיִן etc., but בַּכֶּסֶף, לַכֶּסֶף etc. being much more freq. than בְּכֶסֶף, לְכֶסֶף etc.: for instances in which the art. forms part of the consonantal text, see [Gn 6:20](https://ref.ly/Gen6:20), [7:8](https://ref.ly/Gen7:8) \+ העוף and הבהמה, [Dt 8:3](https://ref.ly/Deut8:3) הַלָּחֶם, [1 K 5:8](https://ref.ly/1Kgs5:8), [5:13](https://ref.ly/1Kgs5:13), [Is 28:7](https://ref.ly/Isa28:7) הַיַּיִן and הַשֵּׁכָר, [60:17](https://ref.ly/Isa60:17), Ez [15:4](https://ref.ly/Ezek15:4), [15:7](https://ref.ly/Ezek15:7) הָאֵשׁ, Hb [2:5](https://ref.ly/Hab2:5), [Pr 20:1](https://ref.ly/Prov20:1), [Psalm 65:14](https://ref.ly/Ps65:14) הַצּאֹן, Ct [1:11](https://ref.ly/Song1:11), [Ec 7:12](https://ref.ly/Eccl7:12). Cf. below, **h. f.** in *comparisons*, the object compared being, as a rule, not an individual as such, but one exhibiting the characteristics of a class: [Is 1:18](https://ref.ly/Isa1:18) כַּשָּׁנִים, כַּתּוֺלָע like scarlet, like crimson (both meant generally), [5:24](https://ref.ly/Isa5:24) כַּמַּק יִהְיֶה, v:[28](https://ref.ly/Isa5:28) כַּצַּר and כַּסּוּפָה, [10:14](https://ref.ly/Isa10:14) כַּקֵּן, [13:8](https://ref.ly/Isa13:8) כַּיּוֺלֵדָה (as always with this word, e.g. [42:14](https://ref.ly/Isa42:14), [Psalm 48:7](https://ref.ly/Ps48:7)), [11:7](https://ref.ly/Isa11:7) כַּבָּקָר; \+ oft. (The usage is not, however, quite uniform, at least acc. to the punctuation: there occurs e.g. כְּקַשׁ [Jb 41:21](https://ref.ly/Job41:21), [Is 47:14](https://ref.ly/Isa47:14); כְּאַרְיֵה Psalm [7:3](https://ref.ly/Ps7:3) al.: and we find both כַּלָּבִיא) [Is 5:29](https://ref.ly/Isa5:29) and כְּלָבִיא [Ho 13:8](https://ref.ly/Hos13:8); כָּאֲרִי [Is 38:13](https://ref.ly/Isa38:13) and כַּאֲרִי [Nu 24:9](https://ref.ly/Num24:9); כַּגִּבּוֺר [Is 42:13](https://ref.ly/Isa42:13) and כְּגבור [Jb 16:14](https://ref.ly/Job16:14); etc.) Similarly [Gn 19:28](https://ref.ly/Gen19:28) כְּקִיטֹר הַכִּבְשָׁן as the smoke of *a* furnace, [Nu 11:8](https://ref.ly/Num11:8), [11:12](https://ref.ly/Num11:12) כַּאֲשֶׁר יִשָּא הָאֹמֵן אֶת־הַיֹּנֵק, [Dt 28:29](https://ref.ly/Deut28:29) (הָעִוֵּר), Ju [8:18](https://ref.ly/Judg8:18) b כְּתֹאַר בְּנֵי הַמֶּלֶח, [14:6](https://ref.ly/Judg14:6) כְּשַׁסַּע הַגְּדִי like (a lion's) rending *a* kid, [16:9](https://ref.ly/Judg16:9), [1 K 14:15](https://ref.ly/1Kgs14:15), [Zc 12:10](https://ref.ly/Zech12:10) כְּ מִסְפֵּד עַל הַיָּחִיד as mourning over *an* only child, [13:9](https://ref.ly/Zech13:9) \+. Where, however, the standard of comparison is not the class in general, but only a particular part of it, defined by a special epithet (whether adj. or verb), the art. is naturally omitted: thus כַּמֹּץ like chaff (in general) [Is 41:15](https://ref.ly/Isa41:15), but כְּמֹץ עֹבֵר like chaff passing away [29:5](https://ref.ly/Isa29:5); כַּצֵּל [Jb 14:2](https://ref.ly/Job14:2), but כְּחֵל נָטוּי [Psalm 102:12](https://ref.ly/Ps102:12); כַּקַּשׁ [Is 40:24](https://ref.ly/Isa40:24), but כְּקַשׁ נִדָּף [41:2](https://ref.ly/Isa41:2); כָּאַיָּל [Is 35:6](https://ref.ly/Isa35:6), but, ´כְּאַיָּל תַּעֲרֹג על וג like *a hind (that) longeth for streams of water* [Psalm 42:2](https://ref.ly/Ps42:2); כָּאֵשׁ [Is 9:17](https://ref.ly/Isa9:17), but כְּאֵשׁ תבער יער like *fire* (*that*) *kindleth a wood* [Psalm 83:15](https://ref.ly/Ps83:15); כֶעָשָׁן [Is 51:6](https://ref.ly/Isa51:6), but כְּעָשָׁן מֵאֲרֻבָּה like *smoke from a chimney* [Ho 13:3](https://ref.ly/Hos13:3): so [Is 62:1](https://ref.ly/Isa62:1) b, [Jb 9:26](https://ref.ly/Job9:26), [11:16](https://ref.ly/Job11:16) כְּמַיִם עָֽבְרוּ like *waters (that) have passed by*, etc.; [Dt 32:2](https://ref.ly/Deut32:2) a כַּמָּטָר, כַּטַּל, but v:[2](https://ref.ly/Deut32:2) b כִּשְעִירִים עֲלֵי־דֶשֶׁא, כִּרְבִיבִים עֲלֵי־עֵשֶב. Where the art. is found, although a rel. clause follows (as [Psalm 1:4](https://ref.ly/Ps1:4), [49:13](https://ref.ly/Ps49:13), [Is 61:10](https://ref.ly/Isa61:10)), this is prob. to be regarded not as *limiting* the class of object compared, but as *describing* it. **g.** prefixed to *generic* nouns (in the singular) it designates the *class*, i.e. it imparts to the noun a *collective* force, as [Ex 1:22](https://ref.ly/Exod1:22) כָּל־הַבֵּן all (lit. the whole of) *the sons*, כָּל־הַבַּת all *the daughters*, [Lv 17:8](https://ref.ly/Lev17:8), [17:10](https://ref.ly/Lev17:10) מִן־הַגֵּר of *the strangers*, who sojourn in their midst, [Nu 21:7](https://ref.ly/Num21:7) הַגנָּחָשׁ *the serpents;* [Gn 14:13](https://ref.ly/Gen14:13), Ez [24:26](https://ref.ly/Ezek24:26), [33:21](https://ref.ly/Ezek33:21) הַפָּלִיט those who escaped; [Jos 6:7](https://ref.ly/Josh6:7) \+ הֶחָלוּץ, v [9:13](https://ref.ly/Josh9:13) הַמְאַסֵּף; [8:19](https://ref.ly/Josh8:19) הָאוֺרֵב; [1 S 13:17](https://ref.ly/1Sam13:17) הַמַּשְׁחִית; [Mi 2:13](https://ref.ly/Mic2:13), [Is 6:4](https://ref.ly/Isa6:4) הַקּוֺרֵא the choir of criers; 1 S 24:l4 הַקַּדְמֹנִי the ancients; [Ec 7:26](https://ref.ly/Eccl7:26) אֶת־הַאִשָּׁה woman, and oft. with gentile names, as הַיְבוּסִי the Jebusites, הַכְּרֵתִי וְהַפְּלֵתִי [2 S 8:18](https://ref.ly/2Sam8:18) al., הַדָּנִי Ju [18:1](https://ref.ly/Judg18:1) etc., הַלֵּוִי the Levites, [Nu 3:20](https://ref.ly/Num3:20), [18:23](https://ref.ly/Num18:23), [Mal 2:8](https://ref.ly/Mal2:8), [Psalm 135:20](https://ref.ly/Ps135:20) \+. **h.** with nouns denoting abstract ideas, esp. the names of moral qualities (cf. Gk. ἡ δίκη, Fr. la justice), chiefly in two cases—(α) where the art. is recognizable in the consonantal text, exceptionally, when some emphasis or definiteness is intended, as [Dt 7:9](https://ref.ly/Deut7:9) שׁומר הברית והחסד; החסד הזה [2 S 2:5](https://ref.ly/2Sam2:5), [1 K 3:6](https://ref.ly/1Kgs3:6); את החסד והרחמים [Je 16:5](https://ref.ly/Jer16:5) (contr. [Zc 7:9](https://ref.ly/Zech7:9)); הצדק † [Is 1:26](https://ref.ly/Isa1:26), [61:3](https://ref.ly/Isa61:3), [Ec 3:16](https://ref.ly/Eccl3:16); הַצְּדָקָה † [Is 32:17](https://ref.ly/Isa32:17), [32:17](https://ref.ly/Isa32:17) (contrast v:[16](https://ref.ly/Isa32:16)) [Dn 9:7](https://ref.ly/Dan9:7) (emph.); הַדַּעַת [Ho 4:6](https://ref.ly/Hos4:6); הָאֱמֶת [Gn 32:11](https://ref.ly/Gen32:11) (sq. אֲשֶׁר), [Is 59:15](https://ref.ly/Isa59:15) (contrast v:[14](https://ref.ly/Isa59:14)) [Zc 8:3](https://ref.ly/Zech8:3), [8:19](https://ref.ly/Zech8:19); הָאֱמוּנָה † [Is 11:5](https://ref.ly/Isa11:5) b (contr. va) [Je 7:28](https://ref.ly/Jer7:28); הַחָכְמָה [Jb 28:12](https://ref.ly/Job28:12), [28:20](https://ref.ly/Job28:20); הַחַיִּים [Gn 2:9](https://ref.ly/Gen2:9) \+; הָאַהֲבָה † Ct [2:7](https://ref.ly/Song2:7), [3:5](https://ref.ly/Song3:5), [8:4](https://ref.ly/Song8:4), [8:7](https://ref.ly/Song8:7); הַשֶּׁקֶר † [Je 7:4](https://ref.ly/Jer7:4), [7:8](https://ref.ly/Jer7:8), [23:26](https://ref.ly/Jer23:26); הַמָּוֶת [Is 25:8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:8), [Ru 1:17](https://ref.ly/Ruth1:17) \+; הָרֶשַׁע † [Psalm 125:3](https://ref.ly/Ps125:3), [Ec 3:16](https://ref.ly/Eccl3:16); הָרִשְׁעָה † [Zc 5:8](https://ref.ly/Zech5:8); הַחשֶׁח [Is 60:2](https://ref.ly/Isa60:2), [Ec 2:13](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:13); see also [Dt 30:15](https://ref.ly/Deut30:15), [30:19](https://ref.ly/Deut30:19) ([Je 21:8](https://ref.ly/Jer21:8)), [1 K 7:14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs7:14), [Je 32:19](https://ref.ly/Jer32:19), [Mal 2:5](https://ref.ly/Mal2:5) החיים והשׁלום, [Psalm 123:4](https://ref.ly/Ps123:4), [130:4](https://ref.ly/Ps130:4), [Dn 9:9](https://ref.ly/Dan9:9), [1 Ch 29:11](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:11), [2 Ch 1:12](https://ref.ly/2Chr1:12), [Pr 31:30](https://ref.ly/Prov31:30), [Ec 2:18](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:18), [2:17](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:17), [7:12](https://ref.ly/Eccl7:12), [7:19](https://ref.ly/Eccl7:19), [10:6](https://ref.ly/Eccl10:6), [11:10](https://ref.ly/Eccl11:10): but in all such cases חֶסֶד, צֶדֶק, אֱמֶת, etc. are far more common. (β) where the art. depends on the punctuation, after preps., esp. בְּ, but with much irregularity, as בַּחֶסֶד [Is 16:5](https://ref.ly/Isa16:5), [Pr 20:28](https://ref.ly/Prov20:28) (but ´בְּח [Ho 2:21](https://ref.ly/Hos2:21), [Pr 16:6](https://ref.ly/Prov16:6)), בַּצֶּדֶק [Pr 25:5](https://ref.ly/Prov25:5) (elsewhere always ´בְּח:בִּצְדָקָה also always); בֶּאֱמֶת and בֶּאֱמוּנָה always; בַּשֶּׁקֶר † [Is 28:15](https://ref.ly/Isa28:15) (but ‖כָּזָב, not הַכָּזָב) [Je 5:31](https://ref.ly/Jer5:31), [13:25](https://ref.ly/Jer13:25), [20:6](https://ref.ly/Jer20:6) [23:14](https://ref.ly/Jer23:14) (בְּשֶׁקֶר † [Je 3:10](https://ref.ly/Jer3:10), [29:9](https://ref.ly/Jer29:9)); בַּשָּׁלוֺם † [Psalm 29:11](https://ref.ly/Ps29:11), [Jb 15:21](https://ref.ly/Job15:21) (elsewhere בְּשָׁלוֺם); [Zc 12:4](https://ref.ly/Zech12:4) to smite בַּשִּׁגָּעוֺן וּבַעִוָּרוֺן (but [Dt 28:28](https://ref.ly/Deut28:28) בְּשׁגעון וּבְעורון), cf. [Gn 19:11](https://ref.ly/Gen19:11), [Dt 28:22](https://ref.ly/Deut28:22); to enter with one בַּמִּשְׁפָּט [Jb 9:32](https://ref.ly/Job9:32), [22:4](https://ref.ly/Job22:4) (but בְּמשׁפט [Psalm 143:2](https://ref.ly/Ps143:2)), contrast also [Pr 18:5](https://ref.ly/Prov18:5) with [24:23](https://ref.ly/Prov24:23); לַמִּשְׁפָּט [Is 59:11](https://ref.ly/Isa59:11), [Psalm 9:8](https://ref.ly/Ps9:8) (but ´לְמ [Is 5:7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:7), [Psalm 122:5](https://ref.ly/Ps122:5)); [Pr 2:2](https://ref.ly/Prov2:2), [7:4](https://ref.ly/Prov7:4) לַחָכְמָה, (but never הַחכמה in Pr 1–9, or indeed in the whole book); [Pr 2:3](https://ref.ly/Prov2:3), [7:4](https://ref.ly/Prov7:4) לַבִּינָה, [Jb 39:17](https://ref.ly/Job39:17) בַּבִּינָה (but never הַבִּינָה); [Pr 2:2](https://ref.ly/Prov2:2), [2:3](https://ref.ly/Prov2:3) לַתְּבוּנָה (but הַתְּבוּנָה only [1 K 7:14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs7:14) emph.); to perish בַּצָּמָא Ju [15:18](https://ref.ly/Judg15:18) \+; [Is 29:21](https://ref.ly/Isa29:21) בַּתֹּהוּ, [32:19](https://ref.ly/Isa32:19) בַּשִּׁפְּלָה תִּשְׁפַּל הָעִיר Germ. in *die* Niedrigkeit sinkt die Stadt, [45:16](https://ref.ly/Isa45:16) together they go בַכְּלִמָּה (in die Schmach), [46:2](https://ref.ly/Isa46:2) בַּשְּׁבִי into captivity, [47:5](https://ref.ly/Isa47:5) בֹּאִי בַחשֶׁח (so always: never בְחשׁך). The living language may have used the art. more readily after a prep., where it did not lengthen the word by an entire syll.; still the disparity of usage between α and β makes it not improb. that the art. in β is in many cases not original but due to the punctuators. **i.** to mark the *vocative:* [1 S 17:55](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:55) חֵי־נפשׁך הַמֶּלֶח as thy soul liveth, *O* king, I do not know, v:[58](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:58) בן מי אתה הַנַּעַר Whose son art thou, *lad ?* [2 S 14:4](https://ref.ly/2Sam14:4) Help, *O* king ! [1 K 18:26](https://ref.ly/1Kgs18:26) הַבַּעַל עֲנֵנוּ, [2 K 9:5](https://ref.ly/2Kgs9:5) אֵלֶיךָ הַשָּר, [Is 42:18](https://ref.ly/Isa42:18) הַחֵֽרְשִׁים שְׁמָעוּ, [Je 2:31](https://ref.ly/Jer2:31) ´הַדַּוֺר אַתֶּם רְאוּ וג, Ez [37:4](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:4) הָעֲצָמוֺת הַיְּבֵשׁוֺת *O* dry bones, [Mal 3:9](https://ref.ly/Mal3:9) הַגּוֺי כֻּלּוֺ (Dr§ 198 *Obs.* 2\).

 N. B. In poetry, the article is frequently dispensed with before words which would naturally take it in prose: thus [Psalm 2:2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:2), [2:8](https://ref.ly/Ps2:8), [2:10](https://ref.ly/Ps2:10) \& oft. אֶרֶץ; [8:9](https://ref.ly/Ps8:9), [18:10](https://ref.ly/Ps18:10), [33:6](https://ref.ly/Ps33:6) \+ שָׁמַיִם; (rarely in prose, [Gn 1:8](https://ref.ly/Gen1:8), [2:4](https://ref.ly/Gen2:4), [1 K 8:35](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:35)); [21:2](https://ref.ly/Ps21:2), [45:14](https://ref.ly/Ps45:14), [45:16](https://ref.ly/Ps45:16), [61:7](https://ref.ly/Ps61:7) al. מֶלֶךְ; [66:6](https://ref.ly/Ps66:6), [72:8](https://ref.ly/Ps72:8) \+ יָם; [59:7](https://ref.ly/Ps59:7), [59:15](https://ref.ly/Ps59:15) עִיר; Ju [5:2](https://ref.ly/Judg5:2), [Psalm 18:44](https://ref.ly/Ps18:44) עָם etc.; [Psalm 9:6](https://ref.ly/Ps9:6), [9:17](https://ref.ly/Ps9:17) \& oft. רָשָׁע; [Is 1:2](https://ref.ly/Isa1:2), [1:6](https://ref.ly/Isa1:6), [1:21](https://ref.ly/Isa1:21), [3:10](https://ref.ly/Isa3:10), [3:11](https://ref.ly/Isa3:11), [11:6](https://ref.ly/Isa11:6), [11:7](https://ref.ly/Isa11:7), [11:8](https://ref.ly/Isa11:8). **2\.** With *adjectives, participles*, and *demonstrative pronouns* (הוא, היא, המה, הם, זה, זאת, אלה): viz. **a.** (so regularly) when the subst. qualified by these words *is defined by it likewise*, as המלך הגדול \= ὁ βασιλεὺς ὁ μέγας, [Gn 2:12](https://ref.ly/Gen2:12) הארץ ההוא, [20:1](https://ref.ly/Gen20:1) הַדָּבָר הַזֶּה, [Jos 2:3](https://ref.ly/Josh2:3) האנשׁים הבאים אליך \= οἱ ἄνδρες οἱ ἐλθόντες πρὸς σέ, [Gn 13:5](https://ref.ly/Gen13:5), [16:13](https://ref.ly/Gen16:13), [24:65](https://ref.ly/Gen24:65), [32:21](https://ref.ly/Gen32:21), Ju [6:28](https://ref.ly/Judg6:28), [Is 65:3](https://ref.ly/Isa65:3), [65:4](https://ref.ly/Isa65:4), [65:5](https://ref.ly/Isa65:5), [66:24](https://ref.ly/Isa66:24), [Psalm 31:9](https://ref.ly/Ps31:9). So also with adjj. \& ptcpp., if the subst. be defined by a sf. (as [Dt 4:37](https://ref.ly/Deut4:37) בְּכֹחוֺ הַגָּדֹל, [1 S 8:14](https://ref.ly/1Sam8:14) זֵיתֵיכֶם הַטֹּבוֺת); and with a pron., if it be defined by a gen. (as [Dt 29:20](https://ref.ly/Deut29:20), [1 S 14:29](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:29), [15:14](https://ref.ly/1Sam15:14), [2 K 6:32](https://ref.ly/2Kgs6:32) בֶן־הַמְּרַצֵּחַ הַזֶּה *this* son of a murderer), but not if it be defined by a sf., as [Ex 10:1](https://ref.ly/Exod10:1) אֹתֹתַי אֵלֶּה (not אתתי הָאֵלָּה), [Jos 2:14](https://ref.ly/Josh2:14) דְבָרֵנוּ זֶה *this* our word (not הַזֶּה ´ד): v. Dr§ 209 *Obs.* Similarly when the art. with the ptcp. has a *resumptive* force, [Psalm 33:15](https://ref.ly/Ps33:15) (v:[14](https://ref.ly/Ps33:14), he looketh forth from the place, \&c.), הַיֹּצֵר יַחַד לִבָּם *he that formeth*, etc., [19:11](https://ref.ly/Ps19:11) הַנֶּחֱמָדִים מִזָּהָב *which* (v:[10](https://ref.ly/Ps19:10) b) *are more desirable* than gold, [49:7](https://ref.ly/Ps49:7). (:[6](https://ref.ly/Ps49:6) b the iniquity of my aggressors surroundeth me), הַבֹּטְחִים *who trust* in their riches, etc., [Gn 49:21](https://ref.ly/Gen49:21), [Is 46:6](https://ref.ly/Isa46:6), [51:20](https://ref.ly/Isa51:20), [Jb 6:16](https://ref.ly/Job6:16) \+ (v. Dr§ 135\. 7\). **b.** (rare) when the subst. qualified by the adj. or ptcp. was felt to be sufficiently definite for its own art. to be dispensed with, as sts. with the word *day* [Gn 1:31](https://ref.ly/Gen1:31) יום השׁשׁי, [2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen2:3) יום השׁביעי, [Ex 12:15](https://ref.ly/Exod12:15), [12:18](https://ref.ly/Exod12:18), [20:10](https://ref.ly/Exod20:10) (\= [Dt 5:14](https://ref.ly/Deut5:14)) [Lv 19:6](https://ref.ly/Lev19:6), [22:27](https://ref.ly/Lev22:27): so with מָנוֺא *entrance* [Je 38:14](https://ref.ly/Jer38:14), שַׁעַר [Zc 14:10](https://ref.ly/Zech14:10); חָחֵר *court* [1 K 7:8](https://ref.ly/1Kgs7:8), [7:12](https://ref.ly/1Kgs7:12), Ez [40:28](https://ref.ly/Ezek40:28) al.; also in certain phrases (peculiar to P) where the subst. is defined by כל, as [Gn 1:21](https://ref.ly/Gen1:21) כָּל־נֶפֶשׁ הַחַיָּה *all* living souls, v:[28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28) כָּל־חַיָּה הָרֹמֶשֶת all living things *that creep*, etc., [7:21](https://ref.ly/Gen7:21), [9:10](https://ref.ly/Gen9:10), [Lv 11:10](https://ref.ly/Lev11:10), [11:46](https://ref.ly/Lev11:46); further in isolated cases, hardly reducible to rule, [Lv 24:10](https://ref.ly/Lev24:10), [1 S 12:23](https://ref.ly/1Sam12:23) דֶּרֶח הַטּוֺבָה, [16:23](https://ref.ly/1Sam16:23) רוּחַ הָרָעָה, [2 S 12:4](https://ref.ly/2Sam12:4) לְאישׁ העשׁיר [Je 6:20](https://ref.ly/Jer6:20), [17:2](https://ref.ly/Jer17:2), [Zc 4:7](https://ref.ly/Zech4:7), [Psalm 104:18](https://ref.ly/Ps104:18), [Ezr 10:9](https://ref.ly/Ezra10:9) חֹדֶשׁ הַתְּשִׁיעִי (quite exceptional in OT). (With prons. this use is so rare that, where it occurs, it is dub. if the text be sound: [1 S 17:12](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:12), [17:17](https://ref.ly/1Sam17:17), [Je 40:3](https://ref.ly/Jer40:3), Kt [Mi 7:11](https://ref.ly/Mic7:11)). And with the ptcp.: [1 S 25:10](https://ref.ly/1Sam25:10) רַבִּים עֲבָדִים הַמִּתְפָּֽרְחִים many are the slaves who break away etc., [Is 7:20](https://ref.ly/Isa7:20), [Je 27:3](https://ref.ly/Jer27:3), [46:16](https://ref.ly/Jer46:16) \= [50:16](https://ref.ly/Jer50:16) חֶרֶב הַיּוֺנָה the oppressing sword, Ez [2:3](https://ref.ly/Ezek2:3) (but LXX Co om. הגוים), [14:22](https://ref.ly/Ezek14:22), [32:22](https://ref.ly/Ezek32:22), [32:24](https://ref.ly/Ezek32:24), [Psalm 62:4](https://ref.ly/Ps62:4), [Pr 26:18](https://ref.ly/Prov26:18), Ju [21:19](https://ref.ly/Judg21:19) (very anomal., rd. prob. לַמְּסִלָּה). This usage is somewhat more freq. in the later parts of OT; and in postB. Heb. it is very general (e.g. יֵחֶד הָרַע the evil inclination): v. further Dr§ 209\. **c.** with the ptcp., where the ptcp. with the art. forms really the *subject:* [Gn 2:11](https://ref.ly/Gen2:11) הוּא הַסּוֺבֵב not ‘ it was encompassing, ’ but ‘ it is *that which encompassed*, ’ [45:12](https://ref.ly/Gen45:12) פִּי הַמְדַבֵּר my mouth is *that which* speaketh, [Dt 3:21](https://ref.ly/Deut3:21) עֵינֶיךָ הָראֹוֺת thine eyes were *those which saw*, [4:3](https://ref.ly/Deut4:3), [8:18](https://ref.ly/Deut8:18) ὅτι οὗτός ἐστιν ὁ διδοὺς σοί, [Is 14:27](https://ref.ly/Isa14:27), [66:9](https://ref.ly/Isa66:9) (v. Dr§ 135\. 7\). **3\.** The article is prefixed exceptionally—mostly in the latest Hebrew—with the force of a relative to the *verb:* † [Jos 10:24](https://ref.ly/Josh10:24) הֶהָֽלְכוּא אִתּוֺ *that* went with him, Ez [26:17](https://ref.ly/Ezek26:17), הָעִיר הַהֻ֫לָּלָה, [1 Ch 26:28](https://ref.ly/1Chr26:28) וְכֹל הַהִקְדִּישׁ שְׁמוּאֵל and all *that* Samuel had dedicated, [29:8](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:8), [29:17](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:17), [2 Ch 1:4](https://ref.ly/2Chr1:4) בַּֽהֵכִין in (the place) *that* he had prepared, [29:36](https://ref.ly/2Chr29:36), [Ezr 8:25](https://ref.ly/Ezra8:25), [10:14](https://ref.ly/Ezra10:14), [10:17](https://ref.ly/Ezra10:17). Acc. to the punctuation, it occurs similarly elsewhere, as [Gn 18:21](https://ref.ly/Gen18:21) הַשָבּאָה (so [46:27](https://ref.ly/Gen46:27), [Jb 2:11](https://ref.ly/Job2:11)), [21:3](https://ref.ly/Job21:3) הַנּוֺלַד־לוֺ, [1 K 11:9](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:9) \& [Dn 8:1](https://ref.ly/Dan8:1) הַנִּרְאָה, [Is 51:10](https://ref.ly/Isa51:10) הַשָּמָ֙ה֙, [56:3](https://ref.ly/Isa56:3) הַנִּלְוָה, [Ru 1:22](https://ref.ly/Ruth1:22), [2:6](https://ref.ly/Ruth2:6), [4:3](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:3) (all הַשָּׁ֫בָה): but in all these passages, the change of a point, or even sts. of an accent, would restore the normal participial construction (as הַבָּשָאה, הַנּוֺלָד לוֺ cf. [Gn 48:5](https://ref.ly/Gen48:5), הַנִּרְאֶה), which is, no doubt, what was intended by the orig. writers, and is recognised elsewhere by the Massorah, e.g. [Gn 12:7](https://ref.ly/Gen12:7), [35:1](https://ref.ly/Gen35:1) הַנִּרְאֶה, [46:26](https://ref.ly/Gen46:26) \& [Ru 4:11](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:11) הַבָּאָ֣ה (cf. Ew§ 331 b Ges138, 3 b). Once, still more anomalously, before a prep. [1 S 9:24](https://ref.ly/1Sam9:24) וְהֶעָלֶיהָ (as though καὶ τὸ ἐπ̓ αὐτῆς): but rd. prob. וְהָאַלְיָה *and the fat tail*, v. Dr. (In Arab. أَلْ also occurs, though very rarely, as a relative: WAG i. § CG 117\).—On the anomalous use of the art. with a word in the *st. c.*, v. ## הֲ (id: BDB02245)

\[Str518,637,1571,3588,3808]הֲ, הַ, •הַ, הֶ (on the different forms, see Ges§ 100\. 4: on [Dt 32:6](https://ref.ly/Deut32:6), v. הַל, p. 210\), **interrog. part.** (BAram. and Targum הֲ, Arab. أَ), prefixed, as a rule, to the first word of a sentence (or clause). **1\.** in *direct* questions: **a.** as a simple interrogative, where the answer expected is uncertain, [Ex 2:7](https://ref.ly/Exod2:7) הַאֵלֵךְ *shall I go* and call thee a nurse ? 1 S 23:1l הֲיַסְגִּרֻנִי *will* the men of Keilah deliver me into his hand ? הֲיֵרֵד שָׁאוּל *will* Saul come down ? [Jb 1:8](https://ref.ly/Job1:8); and frequently. **b.** often in questions, expressed in a tone of surprise, or put rhetorically, to which a *negative* answer is expected (\= Lat. *num ?*): [Gn 4:9](https://ref.ly/Gen4:9) הֲשֹׁמֵר אָחִי אָנֹכִי *Am* I my brother's keeper ? [18:17](https://ref.ly/Gen18:17) *shall* I hide from Abraham that which I am about to do ? [30:2](https://ref.ly/Gen30:2), [50:19](https://ref.ly/Gen50:19), [Nu 11:23](https://ref.ly/Num11:23) a, [Dt 4:33](https://ref.ly/Deut4:33) …הֲשָׁמַע עַם *Did* a people ever hear the voice of God speaking out of the midst of the fire,…and live ? [20:19](https://ref.ly/Deut20:19) (rd. with LXX Targum SyrVer Vulgate Ew Ke Di etc. הֶאָדָם for הָאָדָם), [2 S 7:5](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:5) הַֽאַתָּה תִּבְנֶה־לִּי בַיִת (altered in [1 Ch 17:4](https://ref.ly/1Chr17:4) to the neg. ´לאֹ אַתָּה תִּבְנֶה וג), [2 K 6:22](https://ref.ly/2Kgs6:22) …הַאֲשֶׁר שָׁבִיתָ those whom thou hast taken captive with thy sword and with thy bow, wilt thou smite ? [Is 28:24](https://ref.ly/Isa28:24), [36:12](https://ref.ly/Isa36:12) a, [57:6](https://ref.ly/Isa57:6), [58:5](https://ref.ly/Isa58:5), [Je 15:12](https://ref.ly/Jer15:12), [16:20](https://ref.ly/Jer16:20), [Am 5:25](https://ref.ly/Amos5:25), [Psalm 50:13](https://ref.ly/Ps50:13), [Jb 8:11](https://ref.ly/Job8:11), [15:7](https://ref.ly/Job15:7), [15:8](https://ref.ly/Job15:8), [15:11](https://ref.ly/Job15:11), [38:12](https://ref.ly/Job38:12), [38:16](https://ref.ly/Job38:16), [38:17](https://ref.ly/Job38:17), [38:22](https://ref.ly/Job38:22) etc.; before an inf. absol. (Ew§ 328 a) [Je 7:9](https://ref.ly/Jer7:9), [Jb 40:2](https://ref.ly/Job40:2) and prob. [Mi 2:7](https://ref.ly/Mic2:7) (rd. הֶאָמוֺר: see p. 55\). After a protasis, הֲ…הִנֵה [Nu 22:38](https://ref.ly/Num22:38), [2 K 7:2](https://ref.ly/2Kgs7:2), [7:19](https://ref.ly/2Kgs7:19), [Je 32:27](https://ref.ly/Jer32:27), Ez [17:10](https://ref.ly/Ezek17:10); after הֵן [Je 3:1](https://ref.ly/Jer3:1), [Hg 2:12](https://ref.ly/Hag2:12): cf. after זוּלַי [Gn 24:5](https://ref.ly/Gen24:5); after אִם [Jb 14:14](https://ref.ly/Job14:14) אִם יָמוּת גֶּבֶר הֲיִחְיֶה a man dieth, shall he live ? Occasionally, one or more words precede הֲ (in the same clause) for special emphasis: [2 S 7:7](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:7), [Jb 34:31](https://ref.ly/Job34:31) כִּי אֶל־אֵל הֶאָמַר for *to God* did one ever say…? [Ne 13:27](https://ref.ly/Neh13:27); [Je 22:15](https://ref.ly/Jer22:15) (הֲלאֹ), cf. [23:26](https://ref.ly/Jer23:26). **c.** it is used in questions which, by seeming to make doubtful what cannot be denied, have the force of an impassioned or indignant affirmation: [Gn 27:36](https://ref.ly/Gen27:36) (‘ dubitantis speciem prae se fert Esavus, ut eo acerbius affirmet, ’ Maurer, cited by Ges), [1 S 2:27](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:27) הֲנִגְלהֹ נִגְלֵיתִי וגי *Did I reveal* myself to the house of thy father, when they were in Egypt ? etc. (i.e. of course I did, although thy sons, by their actions, appear to belie it), [1 K 16:31](https://ref.ly/1Kgs16:31) (expressing astonishment), [21:19](https://ref.ly/1Kgs21:19) הֲרָצַחְתָּ וְגַם־יָרָשְׁתָּ *Hast thou* slain, and also taken possession ? [Je 31:20](https://ref.ly/Jer31:20), [Jb 20:4](https://ref.ly/Job20:4), [41:1](https://ref.ly/Job41:1) (cf. Hi); also [Gn 16:13](https://ref.ly/Gen16:13), and the phrases הַרְּאִיתֶם *do ye see ?* [1 S 10:24](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:24) [2 K 6:32](https://ref.ly/2Kgs6:32); הֲרָאִיתָ *dost thou see ?* [1 K 20:13](https://ref.ly/1Kgs20:13), Ez [8:12](https://ref.ly/Ezek8:12), [8:15](https://ref.ly/Ezek8:15), [8:17](https://ref.ly/Ezek8:17) al. **d.** in disjunctive questions, the first question being introduced usually by הֲ, the second by אִם or (more rarely) וְאִם: see exx. under אִם **2** (p. 50\). The disjunctive question may express a real alternative (as [Jos 5:13](https://ref.ly/Josh5:13)), or (as esp. in poetry) the same thought may be repeated in a different form, in two parallel clauses (e.g. [Is 10:15](https://ref.ly/Isa10:15)): in the latter case, the answer *No* is usually expected (v. p. 50\). Only very rarely is the second question introduced by הֲ Ju [14:15](https://ref.ly/Judg14:15) (where, however, הֲלֽם ought no doubt to be read, with MSS., Targum Be al., for הֲלאֹ), or אוֺ Ju [18:19](https://ref.ly/Judg18:19), [Jb 16:3](https://ref.ly/Job16:3), [38:28](https://ref.ly/Job38:28), [38:31](https://ref.ly/Job38:31).

 **2\.** In *indirect* interrogation, *whether* (Germ. *ob*.…?)—**a.** singly, after verbs of *seeing* [Gn 8:8](https://ref.ly/Gen8:8) לִרְאוֺת הֲ to see *whether*, [18:21](https://ref.ly/Gen18:21), [Ex 4:18](https://ref.ly/Exod4:18), Ct [6:11](https://ref.ly/Song6:11), *telling* [Gn 43:6](https://ref.ly/Gen43:6), *trying* [Dt 13:4](https://ref.ly/Deut13:4), *knowing* Ju [3:4](https://ref.ly/Judg3:4), [Ec 3:21](https://ref.ly/Eccl3:21) (rd. הַעֹלָה and הֲיֹרֶדֶת: v. De or Wr *ad loc*.) **b.** disjunctively,—usually אִם…הֲ, [Nu 13:18](https://ref.ly/Num13:18) end, [19](https://ref.ly/Num13:19) a (and see the land) …הֲטוֺבָה הִיא אִם רָעָה *whether* it be good *or* bad, v:[19](https://ref.ly/Num13:19) b, v:[20](https://ref.ly/Num13:20), v:[20](https://ref.ly/Num13:20); אִם לאֹ…הֲ [Gn 24:21](https://ref.ly/Gen24:21), [27:21](https://ref.ly/Gen27:21), [37:32](https://ref.ly/Gen37:32), [Ex 16:4](https://ref.ly/Exod16:4) that I may try it (the people) הֲיֵלֵךְ בְּתוֺרָתִי אִם לאֹ *whether* it will walk in my law or not, [Nu 11:23](https://ref.ly/Num11:23), [Dt 8:2](https://ref.ly/Deut8:2), Ju [2:22](https://ref.ly/Judg2:22); הֲ…הֲ [Nu 13:18](https://ref.ly/Num13:18) (by side of אִם…הֲ); אוֺ…הֲ [Ec 2:19](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:19), [11:6](https://ref.ly/Eccl11:6). It is prefixed to other particles, as הַאִם, הַאַף, הֲגַם, הֲכִי, הֲלאֹ: see אִם, אַף, etc. p. 49, etc. 

> \[Str1973] † הַל [Dt 32:6](https://ref.ly/Deut32:6) הַל יְהוָֺה תִּגְמְלוּ־זאֹת (so V.d. H and other edd.) The reading here became early a subject of dispute, and MSS. and edd. vary accordingly. Some follow the school of Nehardea, and read הַל יְהוָֺה; others (so Norzi) follow the school of Sora, and read הַ לְיהוָֺה; others (so Hahn) have הַלְיהוָֺה. Were הַל original, it would be an interrog. part. \= Ar. ܗܠ, for which elsewhere הֲ is always found. The other punctuations would also each be highly irregular. The true reading is undoubtedly הֲלַיהוָֺה (הַלְיַהְוֶה). Cf. De RossiVar. Lect. *ad loc.*; and on the possible origin of the anomaly, GeiJüd. Ztschr. 1864–65, p. 89 f.

## הֵא (id: BDB02247)

\[Str1887] † הֵא **interj.** (Aram. הָא, [Dn 2:43](https://ref.ly/Dan2:43) הֵא (q.v.), دَرَاء; Arab. ܗܳܐ) lo, behold ! [Gn 47:23](https://ref.ly/Gen47:23), Ez [16:43](https://ref.ly/Ezek16:43).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:23; Ezekiel 16:43; Daniel 2:43


## הֶאָח (id: BDB02248)

\[Str1889] † הֶאָח **interj.** (onomtop.) expressing joy, Aha ! always introduced by אָמַר, [Is 44:16](https://ref.ly/Isa44:16) he says, *Aha*, I am warm, I have seen the fire. Of satisfaction over the misfortune of an enemy or rival, [Psalm 35:21](https://ref.ly/Ps35:21), [35:25](https://ref.ly/Ps35:25) let them not say in their heart הֶאָח נַפְשֵׁנוּ *Aha*, so would we have it ! (*lit* our desire !) [40:16](https://ref.ly/Ps40:16) (\= [70:4](https://ref.ly/Ps70:4)), Ez [25:3](https://ref.ly/Ezek25:3), [26:2](https://ref.ly/Ezek26:2), [36:2](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:2). Metaph., of the neighing of a war horse in the battle, [Jb 39:25](https://ref.ly/Job39:25).

> \[Str2043] הָארָרִי [2 S 23:33](https://ref.ly/2Sam23:33) b v. הֲרָרִי. p. 251

> \[Str3051] הַב v. יהב

> \[Str1890] הַבְהָבִים v. sub יהב. p. 396

## I הֶ֫בֶל (id: BDB02252)

\[Str1892] I. הֶ֫בֶל:73 **n.m.** vapour, breath (NH *id.*, Syr. هَاء *id.*) fig. vanity (so NH, Syr. ܗܰܒܳܠܴܐ)—הָבֶל [Ec 1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2) \+; cstr. הֲבֵל † [Ec 1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2), [1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2), [12:8](https://ref.ly/Eccl12:8), sf. הֶבְלִי etc. [Ec 6:12](https://ref.ly/Eccl6:12), [7:15](https://ref.ly/Eccl7:15), [9:9](https://ref.ly/Eccl9:9), [9:9](https://ref.ly/Eccl9:9); pl. הֲבָלִים [Je 10:8](https://ref.ly/Jer10:8) \+; cstr. הַבְלֵי [Je 8:19](https://ref.ly/Jer8:19) \+, sf. הַבְלֵיהֶם [Dt 32:21](https://ref.ly/Deut32:21) \+;—**1\.** lit. [Is 57:13](https://ref.ly/Isa57:13) all of them (the idols) יִשָּא־רוּחַ יִקַּח הָבֶל a *breath* (Vulgate aura) will carry away, [Pr 21:6](https://ref.ly/Prov21:6) the getting of treasures by a lying tongue is הֶבֶל נִדָּף a *vapour* driven away. Elsewhere always **2\.** fig. of what is evanescent, unsubstantial, worthless, vanity, as of idols [Je 10:15](https://ref.ly/Jer10:15) \= [51:18](https://ref.ly/Jer51:18), [16:19](https://ref.ly/Jer16:19) הֶבֶל וְאֵין בָּם מוֺעִיל (‖שֶׁקֶר) , heathen observances [10:3](https://ref.ly/Jer10:3), and in phr. הָלַךְ אַחֲרֵי הַהֶבֶל [2:5](https://ref.ly/Jer2:5), [2 K 17:15](https://ref.ly/2Kgs17:15); [Pr 13:11](https://ref.ly/Prov13:11) הוֺן מֵהֶבֶל wealth (gotten) *out of vanity*, (i.e. not by solid toil, opp. קֹבֵץ עַל יָד) is minished (but LXX Vulgate Ew מְבֹהָל, v. [20:21](https://ref.ly/Prov20:21) Qr), [31:30](https://ref.ly/Prov31:30) שֶׁקֶר וְהֶבֶל הַ\+יֹּפִי, [La 4:17](https://ref.ly/Lam4:17) אֶל עֶזְרָתֵנוּ הֶבֶל to our *vain* (Dr§ 193 n.) help; of life [Jb 7:16](https://ref.ly/Job7:16) :כִּי הֶבֶל יָמָֽי, [Psalm 78:33](https://ref.ly/Ps78:33) וַיְכַל בַּהֶבֶל יְמֵיהֶם consumed their days *as* (בְּ **1:7 d** *vanity*, man [Psalm 39:6](https://ref.ly/Ps39:6), [39:12](https://ref.ly/Ps39:12), [62:10](https://ref.ly/Ps62:10) :הֵמָּה מֵהֶבֶל יָחַֽד…הֶבֶל בְּנֵי אָדָם they are altogether (made) *of vanity*, [94:11](https://ref.ly/Ps94:11), [144:4](https://ref.ly/Ps144:4), esp in Ec (31 t. \+ הֲבֵל הֲבָלִים [1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2), [1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2), [12:8](https://ref.ly/Eccl12:8)) of the fruitlessness of all human enterprise and endeavour, [1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2) הַכֹּל הֶבֶל, v:[14](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:14) הַכֹּל הֶבֶל וּרְעוּת רוּחַ all was *vanity* and the pursuit of wind, [2:1](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:1), [2:14](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:14), [2:15](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:15) etc., [6:4](https://ref.ly/Eccl6:4) (of an abortion) בַּהֶבֶל בָּא i.e. into a lifeless existence, v:[11](https://ref.ly/Eccl6:11) יֵשׁ דְּבָרִים הַרְבֵּה מַרְבִּים הָבֶל (of discussions leading to no result), note also the phrases יְמֵי (הֶבְלוֺ, הֶבְלְךָ) הֶבְלִי [7:15](https://ref.ly/Eccl7:15), [9:9](https://ref.ly/Eccl9:9), ´יְמֵי חַיֵּי ה [6:12](https://ref.ly/Eccl6:12), [9:9](https://ref.ly/Eccl9:9); [Jb 27:12](https://ref.ly/Job27:12) (v. הָבַל), [Is 49:4](https://ref.ly/Isa49:4) לְתֹהוּ וָהֶבֶל for nought and *vanity* have I spent my strength; as adv. accus. *vainly, to no purpose* [Is 30:7](https://ref.ly/Isa30:7) הֶבֶל וָרִיק יַעֲ\+זֹרוּ, [Psalm 39:7](https://ref.ly/Ps39:7) אַךְ הֶבֶל יֶהֱמָיוּן they disquiet themselves *to no purpose*, [Jb 9:29](https://ref.ly/Job9:29): הֶבֶל אִיגָֽע [35:16](https://ref.ly/Job35:16), with נִחַם to comfort [21:34](https://ref.ly/Job21:34), [Zc 10:2](https://ref.ly/Zech10:2). *Pl.* † הֲבָלִים of false gods, [Dt 32:21](https://ref.ly/Deut32:21) כִּעֲסוּנִי בְהַבְלֵיהֶם ( ‖בְּלאֹ־אֵל) [1 K 16:13](https://ref.ly/1Kgs16:13), [16:26](https://ref.ly/1Kgs16:26), [Je 8:19](https://ref.ly/Jer8:19) הַבְלֵי נֵכָר (‖בִּפְסִילֵיהֶם), [10:8](https://ref.ly/Jer10:8), [14:22](https://ref.ly/Jer14:22) הַבְלֵי הַגּוֺיִם, [Psalm 31:7](https://ref.ly/Ps31:7) הַשֹּׁמְרִים הַבְלֵי־שָׁוְא empty *vanities* [Jon 2:9](https://ref.ly/Jonah2:9); in more general sense [Ec 1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2), [1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2), [12:8](https://ref.ly/Eccl12:8) הֲבֵל הֲבָלִים, [5:6](https://ref.ly/Eccl5:6).

## הָבַר (id: BDB02256)

\[Str1895] † הָבַר **vb.** ? divide (so most, but dub.; Ar. هَبَرَ *cut into large pieces, cut up*, is perh. Denom. fr. هَبْرَةٌ a ‘ chunk ’ of meat; comparison of Ar. خَبُرَ *be acquainted with, skilled in* (AW Kn Di) is also doubtful; if correct, then rd. חבר; but cf. infr.)—**Qal** *Pf.* 3 pl. הברו [Is 47:13](https://ref.ly/Isa47:13) Kt, *Pt.* הֹבְרֵי Qr; הֹבְרֵ֯ו שָׁמַיִם הַחֹזִים בַּכּוֺכָּ בִים *they that divide the heavens, that gaze at the stars* (Kt would be rel. cl. without אֲשֶׁר); ´ה then refers to the distinguishing of signs of zodiac, or other astrological division of sky, cf. Che; on zodiac in Babylon v. JenKosmologie 57 ff; \& on planets \& constellations Idib. 95 ff. Epping \& StrmAstronomisches aus Bab. 109 ff.;—but text prob. corrupt; GFM (June, 1892\) suggests חֹקְרֵי (cf. חקר את־הארץ Ju [18:2](https://ref.ly/Judg18:2)).

BaNB 136\)—only sf. הֲגִיגִי **1\.** [Psalm 5:2](https://ref.ly/Ps5:2) ´בִּינָה ה *understand my murmuring* (whispering, faint utterance), addressed to ´י. **2\.** [Psalm 39:4](https://ref.ly/Ps39:4) בַּהֲגִיגִי *in my musing* i.e. while I was musing.

WeSkizzen iii. 51; Sab. הדד Hal27; as **n.pr.div.** Heb. only in cpd. **n.pr.m.**, v. infr.)—**1\.** name of kings of Edom, **a.** [Gn 36:35](https://ref.ly/Gen36:35) \= [1 Ch 1:46](https://ref.ly/1Chr1:46), [Gn 36:36](https://ref.ly/Gen36:36) \= [1 Ch 1:47](https://ref.ly/1Chr1:47) (הֲדָדָ); **b.** [1 Ch 1:47](https://ref.ly/1Chr1:47) (‖ [Gn 36:39](https://ref.ly/Gen36:39) הֲדַר), v:[51](https://ref.ly/1Chr1:51) (הֲדָדָ). **2\.** an Edomite (הָאֲדֹמִָי) ´ה [1 K 11:14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:14), [11:17](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:17), [11:19](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:19), [11:21](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:21), [11:21](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:21), [11:25](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:25) (הֲדָדָ); \= אֲדַד v:[17](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:17).

## הָדַף (id: BDB02291)

\[Str1920] † הָדַף **vb.** thrust, .push, drive (הֲדַף in Targum; Ar. هَدَفَ is *ingressus* ad aliquem *fuit, propinquus fuit,* also of time *prope accessit*)—**Qal** *Pf.* 3 ms. sf. הֲדָפוֺ [Nu 35:22](https://ref.ly/Num35:22), [Je 46:15](https://ref.ly/Jer46:15); 1 s. sf. וַהֲדַפְתִּיךָ consec. [Is 22:19](https://ref.ly/Isa22:19); *Impf.* 3 ms. יֶהְדֹּף [Pr 10:3](https://ref.ly/Prov10:3); sf. 3 ms. יֶהְדֳּפֶנּוּ [Nu 35:20](https://ref.ly/Num35:20); sf. 3 mpl. יֶהְדֲּפֵם [Jos 23:5](https://ref.ly/Josh23:5); 3 mpl. sf. יֶהְדְּפֻהוּ [Jb 18:18](https://ref.ly/Job18:18); 2 mpl. תֶּהְדָֹּפוּ Ez [34:21](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:21); *Inf.* הֲדֹף [Dt 6:19](https://ref.ly/Deut6:19), [9:4](https://ref.ly/Deut9:4); sf. הָדְפָהּ 2 K [4:27](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:27); —**1\.** *thrust, push,* subj. man, sq. acc. [Nu 35:20](https://ref.ly/Num35:20), [35:22](https://ref.ly/Num35:22) (here fatally); subj. cattle, fig. of men, Ez [34:21](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:21) (instr. בְּכָתֵף; ‖נָגַח); *push away* [2 K 4:27](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:27) (Gehazi, the Shunammite); *thrust away* (fr. oneself) fig. \= *reject,* הַוַּת רְשָׁעִים [Pr 10:3](https://ref.ly/Prov10:3) (subj. ´י). **2\.** *thrust out, drive out,* sq. מִן of place whence \& acc. Pers.; of ´י driving out enemies of Isr. fr. Canaan [Dt 6:19](https://ref.ly/Deut6:19), [9:4](https://ref.ly/Deut9:4), [Jos 23:5](https://ref.ly/Josh23:5); cf. also indef. subj. יֶהְדְּפֻהוּ מֵאוֺר אֶל־חשֶׁח [Jb 18:18](https://ref.ly/Job18:18) (‖וּמִתֵּבֵל יְנִדֻּהוּ); \& [Je 46:15](https://ref.ly/Jer46:15) (where no pl. whence). Hence **3\.** \= *depose* [Is 22:19](https://ref.ly/Isa22:19) of ´י deposing Shebna (sq. מִן of station).

## הָדַר (id: BDB02292)

\[Str1921] † הָדַר **vb.** swell (?), honour, adorn, (NH הִדֵּר *adorn*; Aram. הַדַּר ܗܰܕܰܪ Pa. *adorn, honor;* perh. all denom.; cf. Palm. n.pr.f. הדירת *ornata, honorata* VogNo. 55; Ar.هَدَرَ *be of no account;* but also *ferbuit* (vinum) \& أَهْدَرُ *inflatus, tumens* (venter))—**Qal** *Pf.* 2 ms. וְהָדַרְתָּ֫ [Lv 19:32](https://ref.ly/Lev19:32); *Impf.* 2 ms. תֶּהְדַּר [Ex 23:3](https://ref.ly/Exod23:3), [Lv 19:15](https://ref.ly/Lev19:15); *Pt. pass.* הָדוּר [Is 63:1](https://ref.ly/Isa63:1), pl. הֲדוּרִים [Is 45:2](https://ref.ly/Isa45:2) (poss. הררים LXX ὄρη, cf. Di, but v. הר)—**1\.** *swell*, only pt. pass. pl. [Is 45:2](https://ref.ly/Isa45:2) (si vera l.) in neuter sense, of hills, *swelling places, swells* of land (made level before Cyrus). **2\.** *honour, pay honour to,* sq. acc. ; in good sense וְהָדַרְתָּ פְּנֵי זָקֵן [Lv 19:32](https://ref.ly/Lev19:32) (H) *and thou shalt honour the face of an old* *man* (‖מִפְּנֵי שֵיבָה תָּקוּם); in bad sense (of partiality, favouritism) לאֹ תֶהְדַּר פְּנֵי גָדוֺל [Lv 19:15](https://ref.ly/Lev19:15) (H) (‖לאֹ תִשָּא פְנֵי־דָל); cf. וְדַל לאֹ תֶּהְדַּר בְּרִיבוֺ [Ex 23:3](https://ref.ly/Exod23:3) (JE; either rd. גָּדֹל for ודל,—ו not needed at beginning of v.,—so Kn SS al., yet LXX πένητα;—or suppose balancing cl., as [Lv 19:15](https://ref.ly/Lev19:15), to have fallen out, cf. Di). **3\.** *adorn,* only pt. pass. *adorned, made splendid* הָדוֺר בִּלְבוּשׁוֺ [Is 63:1](https://ref.ly/Isa63:1). **Niph.** *Pf.* נֶהְדָּרוֺ [La 5:12](https://ref.ly/Lam5:12) *were* (not) *honoured,* subj. פְּנֵי זְקֵנִים. **Hithp.** *Impf.* juss. 2 ms. תִּתְהַדַּר [Pr 25:6](https://ref.ly/Prov25:6) *honour oneself,* i.e. *claim honour*.

[Mi 2:9](https://ref.ly/Mic2:9) of ´י's glory as possession of his people, lost by exile \& slavery: but perh. txt. err.; HoffmZAW 1882, 103 prop. הַדְּרוֺר, fr. their children ye take *freedom* (yet מֵעַל then hardly suitable).

> \[Str1927] † \[הֲדָרָה] **n.f.** adornment, glory—only cstr. הַדְרַת;—**1\.** ־קֹדֶשׁ´ה *holy adornment* (ChePsalm), always in connexion with public worship of ´י [1 Ch 16:29](https://ref.ly/1Chr16:29), [2 Ch 20:21](https://ref.ly/2Chr20:21), [Psalm 29:2](https://ref.ly/Ps29:2), [96:9](https://ref.ly/Ps96:9). **2\.** ־מֶלֶח´ה *the glory of a king,* fig. of רָב־עַם, [Pr 14:28](https://ref.ly/Prov14:28).

> \[Str1924] † הֲדַר **n.pr.m.** a king in Edom (Aram. form) [Gn 36:39](https://ref.ly/Gen36:39) \= הֲדַד (q.v.) [1 Ch 1:50](https://ref.ly/1Chr1:50) (so here Codd., SyrVer Sam. no doubt rightly, cf. Di).

> \[Str1913,1913] † הֲדֹרָם **n.pr.m.** an official of Rehoboam [2 Ch 10:18](https://ref.ly/2Chr10:18) (LXX Ἀδωνειραμ) \= אֲדֹרָם [1 K 12:18](https://ref.ly/1Kgs12:18) (LXX Ἀραμ, Ἀδωνιραμ), \& אֲדֹנִירָם q.v.—הֲדוֺרָם v.p.213\.

> \[Str1928] † הֲדַרְעֶ֫זֶר **n.pr.m.** a king of Aram (צוֺבָה) defeated by David [2 S 10:16](https://ref.ly/2Sam10:16), [10:16](https://ref.ly/2Sam10:16), [10:19](https://ref.ly/2Sam10:19) \= [1 Ch 19:16](https://ref.ly/1Chr19:16), [19:19](https://ref.ly/1Chr19:19); also [1 Ch 18:3](https://ref.ly/1Chr18:3), [18:5](https://ref.ly/1Chr18:5), [18:8](https://ref.ly/1Chr18:8), [18:9](https://ref.ly/1Chr18:9), [18:10](https://ref.ly/1Chr18:10) \& הֲדַרְעָזֶר v:[7](https://ref.ly/1Chr18:7), v:[10](https://ref.ly/1Chr18:10); all erron. for הֲדַדְעֶזֶד (q.v.), although LXX throughout Ἁδρααζαρ.

* **Associated Passages:** 1 Chronicles 18:7; Isaiah 2:19; 1 Chronicles 18:9; 1 Kings 12:18; 1 Chronicles 18:5; Psalms 104:1; Psalms 29:2; Micah 2:9; Psalms 111:3; 2 Samuel 10:16; Exodus 23:3; Isaiah 35:2; 1 Chronicles 16:27; Daniel 11:20; Isaiah 45:2; Psalms 96:9; 1 Chronicles 18:8; Psalms 145:12; 2 Chronicles 10:18; Isaiah 2:21; Genesis 36:39; Ezekiel 16:14; Psalms 110:3; Proverbs 25:6; 1 Chronicles 1:50; Psalms 96:6; 1 Chronicles 18:3; Psalms 21:6; Lamentations 5:12; Leviticus 23:40; Ezekiel 27:10; Isaiah 2:10; Proverbs 31:25; Proverbs 20:29; Isaiah 63:1; Lamentations 1:6; Leviticus 19:15; Psalms 90:16; Psalms 45:5; Psalms 29:4; 1 Chronicles 16:29; Proverbs 14:28; 1 Chronicles 19:16; Job 40:10; Psalms 145:5; Psalms 45:4; Psalms 8:6; 2 Chronicles 20:21; 2 Samuel 10:19; Leviticus 19:32; Isaiah 5:14; Isaiah 53:2; 1 Chronicles 19:19; Deuteronomy 33:17; 1 Chronicles 18:10; Psalms 149:9

## הוּא (id: BDB02302)

\[Str1931,1992,2007] הוּא **m.** הִיא **f.** (pl. m. הֵ֫מָּה, הֵם; fem. הֵ֫נָּה, הֵן \[the latter only with prefixes]; see these words), **pron. of the 3rd ps. sing.,** he, she, used also (in both genders) for the neuter it, Lat. *is, ea, id.* (The א is not orthographic merely, but radical, being written on Moab. and Ph. inscriptions, though dropped in some of the later dialects. \[In Heb. only [Je 29:23](https://ref.ly/Jer29:23) Kt, and in the pr. n. אֱלִיהוּ.] Moab. (MI6; MI27) and Ph. (often) הא; Aram. of Zinjirli הא, once הו (DHMInschr. von Sendschirli 55\); Targum הוּא, הִיא Syr. ܗܘ, ܗܝ; Ar. هُوَ, هِيَ (for *hū’a, hī’a,* WSG 104\); Eth. ውእቱ፡ ይእትይ፡ *we’ĕ tū, ye’ĕ tī;* perh. also As. *šû, šî, himself, herself* suff. *šu, ši,* cf. demonstr. *šuatu, šiati* (v. KraetzschmarBAS. i. 383 \& reff., WSG 98, 105 Dl:§ 55b, 57\). In the Pent., הוא is of common gender, the fem. form הִיא occurring only 11 times, viz. [Gn 14:2](https://ref.ly/Gen14:2), [20:5](https://ref.ly/Gen20:5), [38:25](https://ref.ly/Gen38:25) (v. Mass. here), [Lv 11:39](https://ref.ly/Lev11:39), [13:10](https://ref.ly/Lev13:10), [13:21](https://ref.ly/Lev13:21), [16:31](https://ref.ly/Lev16:31), [20:17](https://ref.ly/Lev20:17), [21:9](https://ref.ly/Lev21:9), [Nu 5:13](https://ref.ly/Num5:13), [5:14](https://ref.ly/Num5:14). The punctuators, however, sought to assimilate the usage of the Pent. to that of the rest of the OT, and accordingly wherever הוא was construed as a fem. pointed it הִוא (as a *Qrê perpetuum*). Outside the Pent. the same Qrê occurs [1 K 17:15](https://ref.ly/1Kgs17:15), [Is 30:33](https://ref.ly/Isa30:33), [Jb 31:11](https://ref.ly/Job31:11) a—prob. for the sake of removing gramm. anomalies: five instances of the converse change, viz. of היא to be read as הוּא, occur for a similar reason, [1 K 17:15](https://ref.ly/1Kgs17:15) (וַתּאֹכַל הוּא־וָהִיא to be read as וַתּאֹכַל הִיא־וָהוּא, on account of the fem. verb) [Psalm 73:16](https://ref.ly/Ps73:16), [Jb 31:11](https://ref.ly/Job31:11) b (כי הוא זמה והיא עָוֺן פלילים to be read as כי היא זמה והוא עון פלילים), [Ec 5:8](https://ref.ly/Eccl5:8), [1 Ch 29:16](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:16). The origin of the peculiarity in the Pent. is uncertain. It can hardly be a real archaism : for the fact that arab., Aram., \& Eth. have distinct forms for masc. \& fem. shews that both must have formed part of the original Semitic stock, and consequently of Hebrew as well, from its earliest existence as an independent language. Nor is the peculiarity confined to the Pent.: in the MS. of the Later Prophets, of A.D. 916, now at S. Petersburg, published in facsimile by Strack (1876\), the fem. occurs written הוא (see the passages cited in the *Adnotationes Criticae,* p. 026\). In Ph. both masc. and fem. are alike written הא (CIS i. 1:9 מלך צדק הא, l.:13 מלאכת הא, 3:10 אדם הא, l.:11 ממלכת הא, 93:2, 94:2\), though naturally this would be *read as hu’* or *hi’* as occasion required. Hence, as LXX shews that in the older Heb. MSS. the *scriptio plena* was not yet generally introduced, it is prob. that originally הא was written for both genders in Hebrew likewise, and that the epicene הוא in the Pent. originated at a comparatively late epoch in the transmission of the text—perh. in connexion with the assumption, which is partly borne out by facts (cf. DeZKWL 1880, pp. 393–399\), that in the older language fem. forms were more sparingly used than subsequently.)

 In usage הוּא(f. הִיא; pl. הֵ֫מָּה, הֵם, הֵ֫נָּה : v. הֵ֫מָּה) is **1\.** an emph. he (she, it, they), sometimes equivalent to himself (herself, itself, themselves), or (esp. with the art.) that (those): **a.** [Gn 3:15](https://ref.ly/Gen3:15) הוא ישׁופך ראשׁ *he* (LXX αὐτὸς) shall bruise thee as to the head (opp. to the foll. אתה *thou*), v:[20](https://ref.ly/Gen3:20) for *she* (and no one else) was the mother of all living (so oft. in causal sentences, where some emph. on the subject is desirable as Ju [14:3](https://ref.ly/Judg14:3), [Psalm 24:2](https://ref.ly/Ps24:2), [25:15](https://ref.ly/Ps25:15), [33:9](https://ref.ly/Ps33:9), [91:3](https://ref.ly/Ps91:3), [103:14](https://ref.ly/Ps103:14), [148:5](https://ref.ly/Ps148:5), [Jb 5:18](https://ref.ly/Job5:18), [11:11](https://ref.ly/Job11:11), [28:24](https://ref.ly/Job28:24), [Je 5:5](https://ref.ly/Jer5:5); [34:7](https://ref.ly/Jer34:7) b, [Ho 6:1](https://ref.ly/Hos6:1), [11:10](https://ref.ly/Hos11:10) : Dr:[1 S 14:18](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:18)), [4:20](https://ref.ly/1Sam4:20) Adah bare Jabal הוא היה אבי ישׁב אהלים *he* (ἐκεῖνος) was the father of tent\-dwellers, v:[21](https://ref.ly/1Sam14:21), [10:8](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:8) *he* began to be a mighty one in the earth, [20:5](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:5) (ά̓τός), Ju [13:5](https://ref.ly/Judg13:5), [Is 32:7](https://ref.ly/Isa32:7), [33:22](https://ref.ly/Isa33:22), [2 K 14:7](https://ref.ly/2Kgs14:7), [14:22](https://ref.ly/2Kgs14:22), [14:25](https://ref.ly/2Kgs14:25); [Ho 10:2](https://ref.ly/Hos10:2) *he*—the unseen observer of their thoughts and deeds (Che), [13:15](https://ref.ly/Hos13:15) b (*he,* the foe figured by the E. wind). (For its use thus in circ. clauses v. Dr§ 157, 160, 168, 169\.) And where the predic. is a subst. or ptcp., [Gn 2:11](https://ref.ly/Gen2:11) …הוּא הַסֹּבֵב *that* is the one which encompasseth etc., v:[13](https://ref.ly/Gen2:13), v:[14](https://ref.ly/Gen2:14), [10:12](https://ref.ly/Gen10:12) *that* is the great city. So in the explanatory notices, [Gn 14:3](https://ref.ly/Gen14:3) הוּא יָם הַמֶּלַת *that* is the salt sea, v:[8](https://ref.ly/Gen14:8) הוּא־צֹעַר *that* is Zoar, [36:1](https://ref.ly/Gen36:1) \+ oft. **b.** pointing back to the subj. and contrasting it with something else : [Gn 4:4](https://ref.ly/Gen4:4) הבל גס־הוא Abel, *he* also … v:[26](https://ref.ly/Gen4:26), [10:21](https://ref.ly/Gen10:21), [20:5](https://ref.ly/Gen20:5) וְהִיא־גַם־הִוא and she, *herself* also said, [Ex 1:10](https://ref.ly/Exod1:10) \+ oft. **c.** appended alone to a *verb* (more rarely, but always with intentional emphasis), [Ex 4:14](https://ref.ly/Exod4:14) I know כי דבר ידבר הוא that *he* can speak, v:[16](https://ref.ly/Exod4:16), [1 S 22:18](https://ref.ly/1Sam22:18) ויפגע הוא בכהנים and *he* (though none else would do it) smote the priests, [23:22](https://ref.ly/1Sam23:22) for one hath told me, עָרֹם יַעְרִם הוּא *He* can deal subtilly, Ez [12:12](https://ref.ly/Ezek12:12) (peculiarly), cf. Dr§ 160 n.: very rarely indeed to a noun [Nu 18:23](https://ref.ly/Num18:23) הַלֵּוִי הוּא [Is 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14) הוא ´י, [Est 9:1](https://ref.ly/Esth9:1) (הֵמָּה) being probably all the exx. in the OT. **d.** [Gn 13:1](https://ref.ly/Gen13:1) and Abram came up out of Egypt, הוּא וְאִשְׁתּוֺ *himself* and his wife, and all that he had, [14:15](https://ref.ly/Gen14:15) הוּא וַעֲבָדָיו *he* and his servants, [19:30](https://ref.ly/Gen19:30); so very oft. **e.** *prefixed* to a noun (very rare, and mostly late), [Ex 12:42b](https://ref.ly/Exod12:42) Ez [3:8](https://ref.ly/Ezek3:8) \& [33:8](https://ref.ly/Ezek33:8) הוּא רָשָׁע : to pr. names [Ex 6:27](https://ref.ly/Exod6:27) הוא משׁה ואהרן, [1 Ch 26:26](https://ref.ly/1Chr26:26) *that* Shelomoth, [27:6](https://ref.ly/1Chr27:6), [2 Ch 28:22](https://ref.ly/2Chr28:22), [32:12](https://ref.ly/2Chr32:12) (diff. from [2 K 18:22](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:22)), v:[30](https://ref.ly/2Chr18:30), [33:23](https://ref.ly/2Chr33:23), [Ezr 7:6](https://ref.ly/Ezra7:6): cf. הֵם [Ne 10:38](https://ref.ly/Neh10:38) (comp. in Syr. ܗܘ, Nö§ 227\): cf. [Psalm 87:5](https://ref.ly/Ps87:5), [1 S 20:29](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:29). **2\.** It resumes the subj. with emph.: **a.** when the predic. is a *verb* (esp. if it be separated from its subject by an intervening clause), [Gn 15:4](https://ref.ly/Gen15:4) but one that shall come forth out of thine own bowels, הוּא יִירָשֶָׁךָ *he* shall be thy heir, [3:12](https://ref.ly/Gen3:12) the woman whom thou gavest to be with me, הוא נתנה לי *she* gave to me, [24:7](https://ref.ly/Gen24:7), [44:17](https://ref.ly/Gen44:17) etc. Ju [7:4](https://ref.ly/Judg7:4), [2 S 14:19](https://ref.ly/2Sam14:19) (throwing stress on יוֺאָב) [1 Ch 11:20](https://ref.ly/1Chr11:20), [Is 33:15–16](https://ref.ly/Isa33:15-Isa33:16), [34:16](https://ref.ly/Isa34:16), [38:19](https://ref.ly/Isa38:19), [47:10](https://ref.ly/Isa47:10), [59:16](https://ref.ly/Isa59:16), [63:5](https://ref.ly/Isa63:5), [Ho 7:8](https://ref.ly/Hos7:8); oft. in Pr, as [10:18](https://ref.ly/Prov10:18), [10:22](https://ref.ly/Prov10:22), [10:24](https://ref.ly/Prov10:24), [11:28](https://ref.ly/Prov11:28), [13:13](https://ref.ly/Prov13:13), [19:21](https://ref.ly/Prov19:21), [22:9](https://ref.ly/Prov22:9), [24:12](https://ref.ly/Prov24:12); [1 S 1:13](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:13) (v. Dr), [Psalm 68:36](https://ref.ly/Ps68:36). **b.** when the predic. is a *noun,* [Gn 2:14](https://ref.ly/Gen2:14) and the fourth river, הוּא פְרָת *it* was the Euphrates, v:[19](https://ref.ly/Gen2:19), [9:18](https://ref.ly/Gen9:18), [15:2](https://ref.ly/Gen15:2), [42:6](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6) הַשַּׁלִּיט וְיוֺסֵף הוּא and Joseph, *he* was the ruler etc.: in sentences of the type הוּא הָאֱלֽהִים ´י, הוּא הַנִּלְחָם ´י לָכֶם, הוּא נַחֲלָתְֽךָ ´י, [Dt 3:22](https://ref.ly/Deut3:22), [4:35](https://ref.ly/Deut4:35), [7:9](https://ref.ly/Deut7:9), [10:9](https://ref.ly/Deut10:9), [Jos 13:14](https://ref.ly/Josh13:14), [13:33](https://ref.ly/Josh13:33), [Is 9:14](https://ref.ly/Isa9:14), [33:6](https://ref.ly/Isa33:6), [Ho 11:5](https://ref.ly/Hos11:5) (in these cases, to avoid stiffness, it is convenient often to drop the pron. in translating, as ‘ And the fourth river *was* the Euphrates:’ the pronoun, however, though it then corresponds to the substantive verb in English, does not really *express* it, the copula, as the exx. shew, being in fact understood. Sts. in AV the pron. is retained for emphasis, as Dt. *ll. cc.*) So **c.** after אֲשֶׁר in an *affirmative* sentence, [Gn 9:3](https://ref.ly/Gen9:3) all creeping things אֲשֶׁר הוּא־חַי which are living, [Lv 11:39](https://ref.ly/Lev11:39), [Nu 9:13](https://ref.ly/Num9:13), [14:8](https://ref.ly/Num14:8), [35:31](https://ref.ly/Num35:31) אֲשֶׁר הוּא רָשָׁע לָמוּת who is guilty of death, [Dt 20:20](https://ref.ly/Deut20:20), [1 S 10:19](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:19), [Hg 1:9](https://ref.ly/Hag1:9) al. (On **2,** cf. Dr§ 199, with *Obs.*) **3\.** Where, however, the pron. *follows* the pred., its position gives it the minimum of emphasis, and it expresses (or resumes) the subject as unobtrusively as possible: thus **a.** [Gn 12:18](https://ref.ly/Gen12:18) why didst thou not tell me כי אשׁתך הוא that she was thy wife ? [20:13](https://ref.ly/Gen20:13), [21:13](https://ref.ly/Gen21:13) כי זרעך הוא for he is thy seed, [31:20](https://ref.ly/Gen31:20) because he told him not כִּי בֹרֵחַ הוּא, [37:3](https://ref.ly/Gen37:3) \+ oft. (the opp. order rare and emph.: [Gn 24:65](https://ref.ly/Gen24:65), [Dt 4:6](https://ref.ly/Deut4:6), [30:20](https://ref.ly/Deut30:20), [Jos 10:2](https://ref.ly/Josh10:2), [1 K 2:22](https://ref.ly/1Kgs2:22), [3:4](https://ref.ly/1Kgs3:4), [21:2](https://ref.ly/1Kgs21:2), [Ho 2:4](https://ref.ly/Hos2:4), [Psalm 45:12](https://ref.ly/Ps45:12)). **b.** resuming the subj., [Gn 31:16](https://ref.ly/Gen31:16) all the wealth which God hath taken etc., לנו הוא ולבנינו *it is* ours and our children's, v:[43](https://ref.ly/Gen31:43) and all that thou seest, לי הוא *it is* mine (or, omitting the pronoun, as not required in our idiom, simply) *is* mine, [41:26](https://ref.ly/Gen41:26) חלום פרעה אחד הוא the dream of Pharaoh *is* one, [48:5](https://ref.ly/Gen48:5) (לי הם), [Ex 3:5](https://ref.ly/Exod3:5) for the place where on thou standest, אַדְמַת קֹדֶשׁ הוּא *it* is holy ground, [Nu 13:32](https://ref.ly/Num13:32), [21:26](https://ref.ly/Num21:26), [Dt 1:17](https://ref.ly/Deut1:17), [Jos 5:15](https://ref.ly/Josh5:15), [6:19](https://ref.ly/Josh6:19), [Jb 3:19](https://ref.ly/Job3:19) \+ oft.; [Gn 23:15](https://ref.ly/Gen23:15) מַה־הִוא…ארץ, so [Psalm 39:5](https://ref.ly/Ps39:5), [Is 41:22](https://ref.ly/Isa41:22) (הֵנָּה); הֵמָּה …. אַתֶּם (unusual) [Zp 2:12](https://ref.ly/Zeph2:12). (In all such cases the predicate is not referred directly to the subject, but, the subject being made a casus pendens, it is resumed by the pron., and the pred. thus referred to it indirectly. By this means the sentence is lightened and relieved, esp. if the subject consist of many words: in [Gn 31:16](https://ref.ly/Gen31:16) for instance, the direct form of predicate כִּי לָנוּ וּלְבָנֵינוּ כָּל־הָעשֶׁר אֲשֶׁר הִצִּיל אֱלֽהִים מֵאָבִינוּ would have been heavy and inelegant.) So **c.** after אֲשֶׁר in a *negative* sentence, [Gn 7:2](https://ref.ly/Gen7:2), [17:12](https://ref.ly/Gen17:12) אֲשֶׁר לאֹ מִזַּרְעֲךָ הוּא which is not of thy seed, [Nu 17:5](https://ref.ly/Num17:5), [Dt 17:5](https://ref.ly/Deut17:5), [1 K 8:41](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:41) (cf. הֵמָּה **3 c**). **d.** peculiarly, as the subject of לאֹ, [Je 5:12](https://ref.ly/Jer5:12) לוֺא הוּא *He* is not; and as embracing its predicate in itself, [Is 18:2](https://ref.ly/Isa18:2), [18:7](https://ref.ly/Isa18:7) a nation terrible מִן־הוּא (\= מַאֲשֶׁר הוּא) from (the time that) *it was,* [Na 2:9](https://ref.ly/Nah2:9) מִימֵי הִיא from the days that (*st. c.* Ges§ 130\. 4\) as *it was,* [2 K 7:7](https://ref.ly/2Kgs7:7) they left the camp כַּאֲשֶׁר הִיא as *it was* (cf. כַּאֲשֶׁר הֵמָּה v:[10](https://ref.ly/2Kgs7:10)). (On **3,** cf. Dr§ 198, with *Obs.*) **4\.** It *anticipates* (as it seems) the subject viz. **a.** (rare) Ct [6:9](https://ref.ly/Song6:9) אַהַת הִיא יוֺנָתִי תַמָּתִי one is *she,* my dove my perfect one, [Lv 25:11](https://ref.ly/Lev25:11), Ez [11:15](https://ref.ly/Ezek11:15), [21:16](https://ref.ly/Ezek21:16), [La 1:18](https://ref.ly/Lam1:18) ´צַדִּיק הוּא י (oft. so in NH) ; Ec [6:10](https://ref.ly/Eccl6:10) וְנוֺדָע אֲשֶׁר הוּא אָדָם and that which he, even man, is, is known (De Now); cf. [1 S 6:19](https://ref.ly/1Sam6:19) מִקְרֶה הוּא הָיָה לָנוּ an accident is *it,* (that) hath befallen us. (Cf. הֵמָּה **4 a.** ) **b.** after pronouns—(α) [2 S 7:28](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:28) אַתָּה הוּא הָאֱלֽהִים Thou art *he*—God, [Psalm 44:5](https://ref.ly/Ps44:5) אתה הוא מלבי thou art *he*—my king, [Is 37:16](https://ref.ly/Isa37:16), [43:25](https://ref.ly/Isa43:25) (אנכי), [51:9](https://ref.ly/Isa51:9), [51:10](https://ref.ly/Isa51:10), [51:12](https://ref.ly/Isa51:12), [52:6](https://ref.ly/Isa52:6), [Je 14:22](https://ref.ly/Jer14:22), [29:23](https://ref.ly/Jer29:23) Kt \+; cf. [Je 49:12](https://ref.ly/Jer49:12) וְאַתָּה הוּא נָקֹה תִנָּקֶה and art thou he (that) shall be unpunished ? (with change of Pers. κατά σύνεσιν, cf. Ju [13:11](https://ref.ly/Judg13:11), [1 Ch 21:17](https://ref.ly/1Chr21:17), Ez [38:17](https://ref.ly/Ezek38:17).) So Ew§ 297 b Müll:§ 499\. But others, as GesThes Roo:§ 563 De:Is 37, 16; [Psalm 44:5](https://ref.ly/Ps44:5), treat הוא as emphasizing the pronoun, ‘Thou, he, art God’ i.e. Thou and none else art God; ‘*Thou* (emph.) art my king.’. (β) מִי הוּא, sq. a ptcp. or subst. [Gn 27:33](https://ref.ly/Gen27:33), [Psalm 24:10](https://ref.ly/Ps24:10) מִי הוּא זֶה מֶלֶח הַכָּבוֺד who is he, then—the king of glory ? (acc. to others, as before, ‘*Who* (emph.), then, is the king of glory ?’); sq. a verb [Is 50:9](https://ref.ly/Isa50:9) מִי הוּא יַרְשֹׁיעֵנִי who is he (that) will condemn me ? (al. ‘ *Who* (emph.) will condemn me ? ’) [Jb 4:7](https://ref.ly/Job4:7), [13:19](https://ref.ly/Job13:19), [17:3](https://ref.ly/Job17:3), [41:2](https://ref.ly/Job41:2), [Je 30:21](https://ref.ly/Jer30:21) (so with הֵנָּה [Gn 21:29](https://ref.ly/Gen21:29), הֵמָּה [Zc 1:9](https://ref.ly/Zech1:9), [4:5](https://ref.ly/Zech4:5)). (γ) זֶה־הוּא † [1 Ch 22:1](https://ref.ly/1Chr22:1) Ec [1:17](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:17); (freq. in NH, where the two words coalesce into one זֶהוּ) . On the analogous …אֵלֶּה הֵם), v. הֵמָּה **4 b** (γ). (Cf. Dr§ 200, 201\) **5\.** As an emph. predicate, of God, ‘I am He,’ i.e. I am He Who *is* (opp. to unreal gods, named in context, or to transitory world), the Unseen, yet Omni\-present, and Self\-consistent, Ruler of the world, † [Dt 32:39](https://ref.ly/Deut32:39) אֲנִי אֲנִי הוּא I, I am *he,* and beside me there is no God, [Is 41:4](https://ref.ly/Isa41:4) (v. Che) [43:10](https://ref.ly/Isa43:10), [43:13](https://ref.ly/Isa43:13) even from to\-day I am *he,* [46:4](https://ref.ly/Isa46:4), [48:12](https://ref.ly/Isa48:12), [Psalm 102:28](https://ref.ly/Ps102:28) (v. Che) thou art *he,* and thy years have no end (LXX usu. ἐγώ εἰμι : in Psalm σὺ δὲὁ αὐτὸς εἶ). So also, acc. to many, [Jb 3:19](https://ref.ly/Job3:19), but is הוא *a mere* predicate of identity ? v. rather **3b.** **6\.** In a neuter sense, *that, it* (of an action, occurrence, matter, etc.)—**a.** [Jos 2:21](https://ref.ly/Josh2:21) כְּדִבְרֵיכֶם כֶּן־הוּא acc. to your words, so be *it;* [Gn 42:14](https://ref.ly/Gen42:14) הוא אשׁר דברתי *that* is what I said, [Ex 16:23](https://ref.ly/Exod16:23), [Lv 10:3](https://ref.ly/Lev10:3), [2 K 9:36](https://ref.ly/2Kgs9:36); [Jb 8:19](https://ref.ly/Job8:19) הן הוא משוש דרכו lo *that* (what has just been described) is the joy of his way, [13:16](https://ref.ly/Job13:16), [15:9](https://ref.ly/Job15:9), [31:28](https://ref.ly/Job31:28), [Pr 7:23](https://ref.ly/Prov7:23), [Ec 2:1](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:1), [3:22](https://ref.ly/Eccl3:22), [9:9](https://ref.ly/Eccl9:9), [Est 9:1](https://ref.ly/Esth9:1) b; similarly the fem. הִיא, Ju [14:4](https://ref.ly/Judg14:4) they knew not היא ´כי מי that *it* was from ´י, [Nu 14:41](https://ref.ly/Num14:41), [Jos 10:13](https://ref.ly/Josh10:13), [Is 14:24](https://ref.ly/Isa14:24), [Psalm 77:10](https://ref.ly/Ps77:10) חַלּוֺתִי הִיא *it* (this perplexity) is my sickness, [Jb 9:22](https://ref.ly/Job9:22), [Pr 18:13](https://ref.ly/Prov18:13), [Je 22:16](https://ref.ly/Jer22:16), [2 Ch 25:20](https://ref.ly/2Chr25:20), [Ec 3:13](https://ref.ly/Eccl3:13); ref. to זאת [Am 7:6](https://ref.ly/Amos7:6), [Psalm 118:23](https://ref.ly/Ps118:23), [Jb 5:27](https://ref.ly/Job5:27), זִּה [Ec 2:24](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:24). (Where there is a predicate, the gender of this usually regulates the choice of *m.* or *f.*: hence הִוא [Gn 34:14](https://ref.ly/Gen34:14), [Ex 8:15](https://ref.ly/Exod8:15), [Nu 15:25](https://ref.ly/Num15:25) ([Ec 5:5](https://ref.ly/Eccl5:5)) [Dt 4:6](https://ref.ly/Deut4:6) \+.) **b.** affirming the presence or existence of something (rare) : [2 K 18:36](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:36) \= [Is 36:21](https://ref.ly/Isa36:21) כִּי מִצְוַת הַמֶּלֶח הִיא for *it was* the king's command, saying etc., [1 S 20:33](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:33) (text dub.), [Je 50:15](https://ref.ly/Jer50:15), [50:25](https://ref.ly/Jer50:25), [51:6](https://ref.ly/Jer51:6), [51:11](https://ref.ly/Jer51:11), [Mi 2:3](https://ref.ly/Mic2:3), perh. [Jb 32:8](https://ref.ly/Job32:8). p. 241\. **7\.** With the art. הַהוּא, הַהִיא, הָהֵ֫מָּה, הָהֵם, הָהֵ֫נָּה: so regularly when joined to a subst. defined itself by the art.: [Gn 2:12](https://ref.ly/Gen2:12) הָאָרֶץ הַהִוא *that* land, [19:35](https://ref.ly/Gen19:35) ובלילה ההוא and in *that* night, [21:22](https://ref.ly/Gen21:22) בָּעֵת הַהִוא at *that* time, [Dt 1:19](https://ref.ly/Deut1:19) המדבר הגדול והנורא ההוא. Only four times does there occur the anomalous construction בַּלַּיְלָה הוּא [Gn 19:33](https://ref.ly/Gen19:33), [30:16](https://ref.ly/Gen30:16), [32:23](https://ref.ly/Gen32:23), [1 S 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Sam19:10).* **Associated Passages:** Jeremiah 51:11; ## הָוָא (id: BDB02303)

\[Str1933] † הָוָא **vb.** fall (Ar. هَوَى *id.* e.g. of a star Qor 53:1\)—**Qal** *Imv.* [Jb 37:6](https://ref.ly/Job37:6) for he saith to the snow הֱוֵא־אָרֶץ *Fall* earthwards (an Arabizing usage).

* **Associated Passages:** Job 37:6


## הובנים (id: BDB02304)

\[Str1834] הובנים v. \[הָבְנִי]. p. 211 הוד (√ of foll.; De [Jb 39:20](https://ref.ly/Job39:20) comp. Bed. هَوِيدٌ *crash, roar, resonance;* ‘weithin hallende Selbstverkündigung’ Id. [Psalm 8:6](https://ref.ly/Ps8:6); but هَادَ ordinarily means *be gentle, quiet,* esp. in speech).

> \[Str1935] † I. הוֺד **n.m.**:[Jb 37:22](https://ref.ly/Job37:22) splendour, majesty, vigour (chiefly poet.) (NH *id.*)—´ה abs. [Jb 37:22](https://ref.ly/Job37:22) \+ 8 t. (´הַה [1 Ch 29:11](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:11)); cstr. [Is 30:30](https://ref.ly/Isa30:30) \+ 3 t.; sf. הוֺדִי [Dn 10:8](https://ref.ly/Dan10:8); הוֺדְךָ [Nu 27:20](https://ref.ly/Num27:20) \+ 2 t.; הוֺדֶָךָ [Pr 5:9](https://ref.ly/Prov5:9), [Psalm 145:4](https://ref.ly/Ps145:4); הוֺדוֺ [Ho 14:7](https://ref.ly/Hos14:7) \+ 3 t.; הֹדֹה [Je 22:18](https://ref.ly/Jer22:18) ;—**1\.** *splendour, majesty,* of king הוֺדְךָ וַהֲדָרֶָךָ [Psalm 45:4](https://ref.ly/Ps45:4); הוֺד וְהָדָר תְּשַׁוֶּה עָלָיו [Psalm 21:6](https://ref.ly/Ps21:6), subj. ´י (‖גָּדוֺל כְּבוֺדוֺ בִּישׁוּעָתֶָךָ); foretold of the צֶמַח [Zc 6:13](https://ref.ly/Zech6:13) וְהוּא־יִשָּא הוֺד וְיָשַׁב וּמָשַׁל עַל־כִּסְאוֺ; cf. וַיִּתֵּן עָלָיו הוֺד מַלְכוּת [1 Ch 29:25](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:25) (´י confers on Sol.); ´וְלאֹ נָֽתְנוּ עָלָיו ה מַלְכוּת [Dn 11:21](https://ref.ly/Dan11:21) (of usurper); v. also וְהוֺי הֹדֹה [Je 22:18](https://ref.ly/Jer22:18) in lament for king. Hence also **2\.** divine *splendour, majesty,* light \& glory which God wears as king: הוֺד וְהָדָר לָבָשְׁתָּ [Psalm 104:1](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1) (7 t. joined with הָדָר); cf. [Jb 40:10](https://ref.ly/Job40:10) where ´י addresses Job in irony: עַל־אֱלוֺהַּ נוֺרָא הוֺד [Jb 37:22](https://ref.ly/Job37:22); וְהָדָר לְפָנָיו ´ה [Psalm 96:6](https://ref.ly/Ps96:6) \= [1 Ch 16:26](https://ref.ly/1Chr16:26); also הַגְּדֻלָּה ´וְהַגְּבוּרָה וְהַתִּפְאֶרֶת וְהַנֵּצַח וְהַה [1 Ch 29:11](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:11); see further כִּסָּה שָׁמַיִם הוֺדוֺ Hb [3:3](https://ref.ly/Hab3:3) (‖וּתְהִלָּתוֺ מָֽלְאָה הָאָרֶץ); אֲשֶׁר־תְּנָה הוֺדְךָ עַל־הַשָּׁמַיִם [Psalm 8:2](https://ref.ly/Ps8:2) (‖בְּכָל־הָאָרֶץ מָה־אַדִּיר שִׁמְךָ); also הוֺדוֺ עַל־אֶרֶץ וְשָׁמָיִם [Psalm 148:13](https://ref.ly/Ps148:13) (‖נִשְׁגָּב שְׁמוֺ לְבַדּוֺ); הֲדַר כְּבוֺד הוֺדֶָךָ [145:4](https://ref.ly/Ps145:4); of Yahweh's actions הוֺד וְהָדָר פָּעֳלוֺ [111:3](https://ref.ly/Ps111:3); of his voice אֶת־הוֺד קוֺלוֺ ´זְהִשְׁמִיעַ י [Is 30:30](https://ref.ly/Isa30:30). **3\. a.** *splendour* of Israel under divine blessing וִיהִי כַזַּיִת הוֺדוֺ [Ho 14:7](https://ref.ly/Hos14:7). **b.** *majesty, dignity, authority* of Moses [Nu 27:20](https://ref.ly/Num27:20) (P; only here in Hex) וְנָתַתָּ מֵהוֺדְךָ עָלָיו. Also, **c.** manly *vigour* לַאֲחֵרִים הוֺדֶָךָ פֶּן־תִּתֵּן [Pr 5:9](https://ref.ly/Prov5:9), in warning against licentiousness; as displayed in outward appearance וְהוֺדִי נֶהְפַּךְ עָלַי לְמַשְׁחִית [Dn 10:8](https://ref.ly/Dan10:8) (‖כֹּחַ). **4\.** of a horse, *majesty, majestic force* הוֺד נַחְרוֺ אֵימָה [Jb 39:20](https://ref.ly/Job39:20) *the majesty of his snorting (majestic snorting,* VB) *is a terror;* also in sim. of Yahweh's dealings with Judah, וְשָׁם אוֺהָם כְּסוּס הוֺדוֺ בַּמִּלְחָמָה [Zc 10:3](https://ref.ly/Zech10:3) *and he will make them like the horse of his majesty in battle*.

> \[Str1936] † II. הוֺד **n.pr.m.** (*splendour, vigour*)—a man of Asher [1 Ch 7:37](https://ref.ly/1Chr7:37).

> \[Str1937,1938] † הודוה Kt, חוֺדְיָה Qr, **n.pr.m.** only in ´בְּנֵי לְה [Ne 7:43](https://ref.ly/Neh7:43) of a Levitical family; \= הוֺדַוְיָה [Ezr 2:40](https://ref.ly/Ezra2:40) q.v. sub ידה. p. 392

> \[Str1940–41] † הוֺדִיָּה **n.pr.m.** (*my splendour is Yah*)—**1\.** a man of Judah [1 Ch 4:19](https://ref.ly/1Chr4:19). **2\.** Levites **a.** [Ne 8:7](https://ref.ly/Neh8:7), [9:5](https://ref.ly/Neh9:5), [10:11](https://ref.ly/Neh10:11); **b.** [Ne 10:14](https://ref.ly/Neh10:14). **3\.** a chief of the people [Ne 10:19](https://ref.ly/Neh10:19).

> \[Str1937] † הוֺדְיָה Qr [Ne 7:43](https://ref.ly/Neh7:43) v. הודוה supr.

> \[Str1938–39] † הוֺדַוְיָה, הוֺדַוְיָהוּ, הודיוהו **n.pr.m.** v. sub ידה. p. 392f

## הָוָה (id: BDB02311)

\[Str1933] † \[הָוָה] **vb.** become (Ar. هَوَى *to fall* (v. הָוָא), also *to gape* or *yawn*, and *to desire* (cf. הַוָּה): cf. FlDe [Jb 6:2](https://ref.ly/Job6:2); Aram. הֲוָא, ܗܘܳܐ the usual word for *to be* (prob. orig. *to fall out, accidit,* hence *come to pass, come to be,* γίγνεσθαι), Mish. *id.* very common) a rare syn. of הָיָה q.v. :—**Qal** *Impf.* apoc. יְהוּא [Ec 11:3](https://ref.ly/Eccl11:3) (for יְהוּ with א *otiosum*; Ges§ 75 R I 3 e Könp. 597 f.; but Gr. plausibly הוּא); *Imv.* ms. הֱוֵה [Gn 27:29](https://ref.ly/Gen27:29), fs., הֱוִי [Is 16:4](https://ref.ly/Isa16:4), *Pt.* הֹוֶה [Ec 2:22](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:22), [Ne 6:6](https://ref.ly/Neh6:6) :—[Gn 27:29](https://ref.ly/Gen27:29) הֱוֵה גְבִיר לְאַחֶיךָ *become* lord to thy brethren, [Is 16:4](https://ref.ly/Isa16:4) (perh. in imit. of Moab. dialect) הֱוִי סֵתֶר לָמוֺ *become* thou (Zion) a defence to them, [Ec 2:22](https://ref.ly/Eccl2:22), [11:3](https://ref.ly/Eccl11:3), [Ne 6:6](https://ref.ly/Neh6:6).

> \[Str1942] † הַוָּה **n.f. 1\.** desire ; **2\.** chasm, fig. destruction (cf. هَوًى *desire*, هَوَاءٌ *atmosphere, emptiness*, هَاوِيَةٌ *a deep pit, hell* (cf. sub הָוָה); Aram. ܗܰܘܬܴܐ *gulf, chasm* ([Lu 16:26](https://ref.ly/Luke16:26) SyrVer)) cstr. הַוַּת [Mi 7:3](https://ref.ly/Mic7:3) \+, sf. הַוָּתִי [Jb 6:2](https://ref.ly/Job6:2) \+, pl. הַוּוֺת [Psalm 5:10](https://ref.ly/Ps5:10) \+ ;—**1\.** *desire* (in bad sense) [Mi 7:3](https://ref.ly/Mic7:3) דֹּבֵר הַוַּת נַפְשׁוֺ speaketh *the desire of* his soul, [Pr 10:3](https://ref.ly/Prov10:3) הַוַּת רְשָׁעִים, [11:6](https://ref.ly/Prov11:6) בֹּגְדִים ´ה, [Psalm 52:9](https://ref.ly/Ps52:9) יָעֹז בְּהַוָּתוֺ became strong through his (evil) *desire* (but read rather with SyrVer Targum Lag Gr BI Che Now בְּהוֺנוֺ : ‖בְּעָשְׁרוֺ). **2\.** *engulfing ruin, destruction* [Jb 6:2](https://ref.ly/Job6:2), Qr [30:13](https://ref.ly/Job30:13) (cf. Baer's note) הַוָּתִי of Job's great calamity; pl. (intens.) הַוּוֺת, esp. as wrought, or meditated, by one against another, [Psalm 5:10](https://ref.ly/Ps5:10) קִרְבָּם הַוּוֺת their inward part is *engulfing ruin* (Che), [38:13](https://ref.ly/Ps38:13) דִּבְּרוּ הַוּוֺת, [52:4](https://ref.ly/Ps52:4) תַּחֲשֹׁב ´ה לְשׁוֺנְךָ (cf. [Pr 17:4](https://ref.ly/Prov17:4) לְשׁוֺן הַוּוֺת), [55:12](https://ref.ly/Ps55:12) בְּקִרְבָּהּ ´ה, [57:2](https://ref.ly/Ps57:2) ´עַד יַעֲבֹר ה till *the storm of ruin* (Che) pass by, [91:3](https://ref.ly/Ps91:3) ´דֶּבֶר ה *destructive* pestilence, [94:20](https://ref.ly/Ps94:20) ´כִּסֵּא ה the seat (tribunal) of destruction (i.e. which ruins the innocent by injustice), [Pr 19:13](https://ref.ly/Prov19:13) a foolish son is הַוּוֺת לְאָבָיו, [Jb 6:30](https://ref.ly/Job6:30) (cf. v:[2](https://ref.ly/Job6:2) supr.)

> \[Str1962] הַיָּה **n.f.** destruction, sf. הַיָּתִי [Jb 6:2](https://ref.ly/Job6:2) Kt, i.q. הַוָּתִי (Qr), and prob. an error for it: v. הַוָּה **2\.** p. 228

> \[Str1943] † הֹוָה **n.f.** ruin, disaster (cf. sub הַוָּה) Ez [7:26](https://ref.ly/Ezek7:26) הֹוָה עַל הֹוָה תָבוֺא *disaster* shall come upon *disaster,* [Is 47:11](https://ref.ly/Isa47:11) וְתִפֹּל עָלַיִח הֹוָה *disaster* shall fall upon thee (‖רָעָה, שֹׁאָה).

> \[Str3068–69,1961,430,589,2022,6635] יהוה:c. 6823 i.e. יַהְוֶה **n.pr.dei** Yahweh, the proper name of the God of Israel—(**1\.** MT יְהוָֺה:C518 (Qr אֲדֹנָי), or יֱהוִֺה:305 (Qr אֱלֽהִים), in the combinations אדני יהוה \& יהוה אדני (vid. אֲדֹנֶי), and with prep. בַּיהוָֺה, לַיהוָֺה, מֵיהוָֺה (Qr בַּאדֹנָי, לַאדֹנָי, מֵאדֹנָי), do not give the original form. LXX and other Vrss follow the Qr. On the basis of [Ex 20:7](https://ref.ly/Exod20:7), [Lv 24:11](https://ref.ly/Lev24:11) יהוה was regarded as a *nomen ineffabile* (vid. Philode Vita Mosis iii. 519, 529\), called by the Jews הַשֵּׁם and by the Samaritans שׁימא. The pronunciation *Jehovah* was unknown until 1520, when it was introduced by Galatinus; but it was contested by Le Mercier, J. Drusius, and L. Capellus, as against grammatical and historical propriety (cf. Bö§ 88\). The traditional Ἰαβέ of Theodoret and Epiphanius, the ־יָהוּ, יְה־וֺ of compound n.pr. and the contracted form יָהּ all favour יַהְוֶךְ (cf. יַהֲלֽמָוּן [Psalm 74:6](https://ref.ly/Ps74:6); תַּהֲרוּ [Is 33:11](https://ref.ly/Isa33:11)), v.LagSym i.14 BaudissinStudien i.179 ff.; DrStud.Bib.i.1 ff. For *Jeve* v. StaZAW 1881, 346 Deib.1882, 173 f. \& Gn. Excurs. ii. **2\.** on liter. of interpret. v. NesEg.67 Drl.c.—Many recent scholars explain יַהְוֶה as Hiph. of היה (\= היה) *the one bringing into being, life\-giver* (cf. חַוָּה [Gn 3:20](https://ref.ly/Gen3:20)) Schr HSch; *giver of existence, creator,* Kue Tiele; *he who brings to pass* (so already Le Clerc),*performer of his promises,* Lag, NesEg.88 (but NesEg.91 inclines to Qal as RSBrit. \& For. Ev. Rev v. infr.); or from היה *he who causes to fall,* rain or lightning RSOTJC.ed.1, 423; om.ed.2, 245, cf. WeSkizzen iii.175; ‘*Fäller,*’ destroying foes, StaG.i.429 (dubiously). But most take it as Qal of הוה (\= היה); *the one who is*: i.e. *the absolute and unchangeable one,* Ri ; the *existing, ever living,* as self\-consistent and unchangeable, Di; or *the one ever coming into manifestation* as the God of redemption, De Oehl; cf. also RSBrit. \& For. Ev. Rev. 1876, *he will be it,* i.e all that his servants look for (cf. Ewinfr), *he will approve himself (give evidence of being, assert his being Drl.c.17\)*).
> 
>  *Note.*—BonkZAW 1891, 126 ff. seems to shew that as prefix, in comp. n.pr., יְהוֺ is the oldest and the latest form and that יוֺ is intermediate, belonging to the earlier post\-exilic period until the time of Chr; occasional copyists’ mistakes being taken into the account.

> \[Str3050] יָהּ:50 **n.pr.dei** contr. fr. יהוה, first appears in early poems; [Ex 15:2](https://ref.ly/Exod15:2) עָוִּי וְזִמְרָת יָהּ *My strength and song is Yah* (cited [Is 12:2](https://ref.ly/Isa12:2), [Psalm 118:14](https://ref.ly/Ps118:14)), cf. the poetic extract יָד עַל כֵּס יָהּ \= *hand to the throne of Yah* [Ex 17:16](https://ref.ly/Exod17:16) (E), the song of Hezekiah [Is 38:11](https://ref.ly/Isa38:11) (repeated by dittography), שׁ להבת יה(א) (so read in preference to the MT שַׁלְהֶבֶתְיָה) \= *flame of fire from Yah* Ct [8:6](https://ref.ly/Song8:6); ביה יהוה [Is 26:4](https://ref.ly/Isa26:4) (והיה sustained by Aq and the rhythmical movement, unless it be a mistake for שׁמו, cf. [Psalm 68:5](https://ref.ly/Ps68:5)), יה אלהים [Psalm 68:19](https://ref.ly/Ps68:19). Elsewhere יָהּ is used only in late Psalm, especially in the Hallels, in the phrase הַלֲלוּ־יָהּ *praise ye Yah* [Psalm 104:35](https://ref.ly/Ps104:35), [105:45](https://ref.ly/Ps105:45), [106:1](https://ref.ly/Ps106:1), [106:48](https://ref.ly/Ps106:48), [111:1](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1), [112:1](https://ref.ly/Ps112:1), [113:1](https://ref.ly/Ps113:1), [113:9](https://ref.ly/Ps113:9), [115:18](https://ref.ly/Ps115:18), [116:19](https://ref.ly/Ps116:19), [117:2](https://ref.ly/Ps117:2), [135:1](https://ref.ly/Ps135:1), [135:3](https://ref.ly/Ps135:3), [135:21](https://ref.ly/Ps135:21), [146:1](https://ref.ly/Ps146:1), [146:10](https://ref.ly/Ps146:10), [147:1](https://ref.ly/Ps147:1), [147:20](https://ref.ly/Ps147:20), [148:1](https://ref.ly/Ps148:1), [148:14](https://ref.ly/Ps148:14), [149:1](https://ref.ly/Ps149:1), [149:9](https://ref.ly/Ps149:9), [150:1](https://ref.ly/Ps150:1), [150:6](https://ref.ly/Ps150:6), cf. also יהלל יה [102:19](https://ref.ly/Ps102:19) יהללו יה [115:17](https://ref.ly/Ps115:17) תהלל יה [Psalm 150:6](https://ref.ly/Ps150:6) (v. הלל); in var. other phrases [Psalm 77:12](https://ref.ly/Ps77:12), [89:9](https://ref.ly/Ps89:9), [94:7](https://ref.ly/Ps94:7), [94:12](https://ref.ly/Ps94:12), [115:18](https://ref.ly/Ps115:18), [118:5](https://ref.ly/Ps118:5), [118:5](https://ref.ly/Ps118:5), [118:17](https://ref.ly/Ps118:17), [118:18](https://ref.ly/Ps118:18), [118:19](https://ref.ly/Ps118:19), [122:4](https://ref.ly/Ps122:4), [130:3](https://ref.ly/Ps130:3), [135:4](https://ref.ly/Ps135:4).
